1	# Retrospective Workflow
2	
3	**Goal:** Post-epic review to extract lessons and assess success.
4	
5	**Your Role:** Developer facilitating retrospective.
6	- No time estimates — NEVER mention hours, days, weeks, months, or ANY time-based predictions. AI has fundamentally changed development speed.
7	- Communicate all responses in {communication_language} and language MUST be tailored to {user_skill_level}
8	- Generate all documents in {document_output_language}
9	- Document output: Retrospective analysis. Concise insights, lessons learned, action items. User skill level ({user_skill_level}) affects conversation style ONLY, not retrospective content.
10	- Facilitation notes:
11	  - Psychological safety is paramount - NO BLAME
12	  - Focus on systems, processes, and learning
13	  - Everyone contributes with specific examples preferred
14	  - Action items must be achievable with clear ownership
15	  - Two-part format: (1) Epic Review + (2) Next Epic Preparation
16	- Party mode protocol:
17	  - ALL agent dialogue MUST use format: "Name (Role): dialogue"
18	  - Example: Amelia (Developer): "Let's begin..."
19	  - Example: {user_name} (Project Lead): [User responds]
20	  - Create natural back-and-forth with user actively participating
21	  - Show disagreements, diverse perspectives, authentic team dynamics
22	
23	---
24	
25	## INITIALIZATION
26	
27	### Configuration Loading
28	
29	Load config from `{project-root}/_bmad/bmm/config.yaml` and resolve:
30	
31	- `project_name`, `user_name`
32	- `communication_language`, `document_output_language`
33	- `user_skill_level`
34	- `planning_artifacts`, `implementation_artifacts`
35	- `date` as system-generated current datetime
36	- YOU MUST ALWAYS SPEAK OUTPUT in your Agent communication style with the config `{communication_language}`
37	
38	### Paths
39	
40	- `sprint_status_file` = `{implementation_artifacts}/sprint-status.yaml`
41	
42	### Input Files
43	
44	| Input | Description | Path Pattern(s) | Load Strategy |
45	|-------|-------------|------------------|---------------|
46	| epics | The completed epic for retrospective | whole: `{planning_artifacts}/*epic*.md`, sharded_index: `{planning_artifacts}/*epic*/index.md`, sharded_single: `{planning_artifacts}/*epic*/epic-{{epic_num}}.md` | SELECTIVE_LOAD |
47	| previous_retrospective | Previous epic's retrospective (optional) | `{implementation_artifacts}/**/epic-{{prev_epic_num}}-retro-*.md` | SELECTIVE_LOAD |
48	| architecture | System architecture for context | whole: `{planning_artifacts}/*architecture*.md`, sharded: `{planning_artifacts}/*architecture*/*.md` | FULL_LOAD |
49	| prd | Product requirements for context | whole: `{planning_artifacts}/*prd*.md`, sharded: `{planning_artifacts}/*prd*/*.md` | FULL_LOAD |
50	| document_project | Brownfield project documentation (optional) | sharded: `{planning_artifacts}/*.md` | INDEX_GUIDED |
51	
52	### Required Inputs
53	
54	- `agent_manifest` = `{project-root}/_bmad/_config/agent-manifest.csv`
55	
56	### Context
57	
58	- `project_context` = `**/project-context.md` (load if exists)
59	
60	---
61

210	<action>Extract and analyze from each story:</action>
211	
212	**Dev Notes and Struggles:**
213	
214	- Look for sections like "## Dev Notes", "## Implementation Notes", "## Challenges", "## Development Log"
215	- Identify where developers struggled or made mistakes
216	- Note unexpected complexity or gotchas discovered
217	- Record technical decisions that didn't work out as planned
218	- Track where estimates were way off (too high or too low)
219	
220	**Review Feedback Patterns:**
221	
222	- Look for "## Review", "## Code Review", "## Dev Review" sections
223	- Identify recurring feedback themes across stories
224	- Note which types of issues came up repeatedly
225	- Track quality concerns or architectural misalignments
226	- Document praise or exemplary work called out in reviews
227	
228	**Lessons Learned:**
229	
230	- Look for "## Lessons Learned", "## Retrospective Notes", "## Takeaways" sections within stories
231	- Extract explicit lessons documented during development
232	- Identify "aha moments" or breakthroughs
233	- Note what would be done differently
234	- Track successful experiments or approaches
235	
236	**Technical Debt Incurred:**
237	
238	- Look for "## Technical Debt", "## TODO", "## Known Issues", "## Future Work" sections
239	- Document shortcuts taken and why
240	- Track debt items that affect next epic
241	- Note severity and priority of debt items
242	
243	**Testing and Quality Insights:**
244	
245	- Look for "## Testing", "## QA Notes", "## Test Results" sections
246	- Note testing challenges or surprises
247	- Track bug patterns or regression issues
248	- Document test coverage gaps
249	
250	<action>Synthesize patterns across all stories:</action>
251	
252	**Common Struggles:**
253	
254	- Identify issues that appeared in 2+ stories (e.g., "3 out of 5 stories had API authentication issues")
255	- Note areas where team consistently struggled
256	- Track where complexity was underestimated
257	
258	**Recurring Review Feedback:**
259	
260	- Identify feedback themes (e.g., "Error handling was flagged in every review")
261	- Note quality patterns (positive and negative)
262	- Track areas where team improved over the course of epic
263	
264	**Breakthrough Moments:**
265	
266	- Document key discoveries (e.g., "Story 3 discovered the caching pattern we used for rest of epic")
267	- Note when team velocity improved dramatically
268	- Track innovative solutions worth repeating
269	
270	**Velocity Patterns:**
271	
272	- Calculate average completion time per story
273	- Note velocity trends (e.g., "First 2 stories took 3x longer than estimated")
274	- Identify which types of stories went faster/slower
275	
276	**Team Collaboration Highlights:**
277	
278	- Note moments of excellent collaboration mentioned in stories
279	- Track where pair programming or mob programming was effective
280	- Document effective problem-solving sessions
281

708	## Epic 6: Post-v1.0.0 Cleanup
709	
710	Repo no longer carries the legacy workflows, scripts, or tokens once the canonical OIDC path is proven by the shipped v1.0.0.
711	
712	### Story 6.1: Delete legacy `publish.yaml` and `manual-release.yaml` workflows
713	
714	As the maintainer,
715	I want the deprecated legacy workflows removed from `.github/workflows/`,
716	So that the repo's release surface is physically single-source-of-truth and no operator (human or bot) can accidentally invoke an inert path.
717	
718	**Acceptance Criteria:**
719	
720	**Given** v1.0.0 is successfully published (Epic 5 complete) and the release-audit artifact is closed-green
721	**When** I open a PR removing the legacy workflows
722	**Then** `.github/workflows/publish.yaml` SHALL be deleted
723	**And** `.github/workflows/manual-release.yaml` SHALL be deleted
724	**And** the PR description SHALL reference FR19 and cite the Epic 5 close-out as the enabling precondition
725	
726	**Given** the PR is opened
727	**When** CI runs on it
728	**Then** all required status checks (`quality.yaml`) SHALL pass
729	**And** the PR SHALL be reviewed and approved per Story 1.1 branch protection
730	
731	**Given** the PR is merged
732	**When** I verify GitHub Actions UI
733	**Then** `publish.yaml` and `manual-release.yaml` SHALL no longer appear in the workflow list
734	**And** `release.yaml` SHALL remain the only release-related workflow on `main`
735	
736	**Given** legacy workflows have been deleted
737	**When** someone attempts to trigger them by name
738	**Then** the attempt SHALL fail (workflow not found) — confirming no orphaned triggers exist
739	
740	### Story 6.2: Remove `release:*` scripts from `package.json`
741	
742	As the maintainer,
743	I want the deprecated `release:*` scripts removed from `package.json`,
744	So that the npm script surface reflects the actual supported operations and no one is tempted to bypass the canonical CI path.
745	
746	**Acceptance Criteria:**
747	
748	**Given** Story 4.2 had prefixed `release:*` scripts with a fail-loud deprecation notice
749	**When** I open a PR removing the scripts entirely
750	**Then** every script key matching `release:*` (e.g., `release:patch`, `release:minor`, `release:major`, `release:alpha`, `release:beta`) SHALL be deleted from `package.json`
751	
752	**Given** the scripts are removed
753	**When** CI runs on the PR
754	**Then** `npm test` SHALL still pass (no test depends on `release:*`)
755	**And** the lockfile SHALL remain unchanged (these are script entries, not dependencies)
756	
757	**Given** the PR is merged
758	**When** a user runs `npm run release:patch` (or any legacy invocation)
759	**Then** npm SHALL report "Missing script: release:patch" — confirming removal
760	
761	**Given** Story 4.2's deprecation prefixes referenced `docs/RELEASING.md`
762	**When** I verify
763	**Then** `docs/RELEASING.md` (from Story 4.1) SHALL remain the canonical reference and SHALL describe only the `release.yaml`-via-GitHub-Actions path (no mention of `release:*` scripts as a supported method)
764	
765	### Story 6.3: Remove `NPM_TOKEN` secret from repo settings
766	
767	As the maintainer,
768	I want the `NPM_TOKEN` repo secret removed now that OIDC trusted publishing has shipped v1.0.0 successfully,
769	So that SKF no longer carries a long-lived npm credential capable of publishing — closing the attack surface that OIDC was migrated to eliminate.
770	
771	**Acceptance Criteria:**
772	
773	**Given** v1.0.0 was published successfully via OIDC (Story 5.3 verified, Story 5.4 closed)
774	**When** I go to Repo Settings → Secrets and variables → Actions
775	**Then** I SHALL delete the `NPM_TOKEN` repo-level secret
776	
777	**Given** the secret has been removed
778	**When** a future release workflow runs
779	**Then** it SHALL continue to publish successfully via OIDC (the workflow uses `NPM_TOKEN: ""` per Story 3.1 and never reads the secret)
780	**And** the absence of the secret SHALL NOT cause any CI failure
781	
782	**Given** the secret has been removed
783	**When** I verify the broader audit surface
784	**Then** the `release` GitHub Environment SHALL still contain no `NPM_TOKEN` (Story 1.2 ensured it was never scoped there)
785	**And** no workflow file on `main` SHALL still reference `secrets.NPM_TOKEN` (confirms Story 6.1 removed the legacy workflows that consumed it)
786	
787	**Given** all three cleanup stories (6.1, 6.2, 6.3) are complete
788	**When** I close the v1.0.0 launch audit artifact's cleanup section
789	**Then** the following SHALL be true of the repo state:
790	- Single canonical workflow at `.github/workflows/release.yaml`
791	- No `release:*` scripts in `package.json`
792	- No `NPM_TOKEN` secret in repo settings
793	- No long-lived npm publish credential exists anywhere in SKF's infrastructure
794	
795	**Given** the cleanup is complete
796	**When** I commit a final summary
797	**Then** the Epic 6 close-out SHALL note: "SKF's npm publish credential surface is now OIDC-only. Future incidents requiring credential revocation involve npmjs.com's trusted-publisher config, not a repo secret."
798

1	# Epic 5 Retrospective — v1.0.0 Release Candidate Cycle
2	
3	**Date:** 2026-04-24
4	**Facilitator:** bmad-retrospective (Amelia)
5	**Project Lead:** Armel
6	**Epic status at retro:** all 4 stories `done`; `epic-5: done` in `sprint-status.yaml` (already flipped via Story 5.4 code-review close-out); `epic-5-retrospective: optional → done` as part of this retro. Stale `epic-3 / 3-5 in-progress` entries flipped to `done` as retro housekeeping — Story 3-5 met its own "transition to done" condition (AC #8 E2E validation via Story 5.2 rc.3 + Story 5.3 v1.0.0 success).
7	**Previous retrospective:** Epic 4 (2026-04-23).
8	
9	## Summary
10	
11	**v1.0.0 LAUNCHED on npm under `--tag latest` at 2026-04-23T18:56:39Z** with OIDC trusted publishing, SLSA L2 provenance, `isPrerelease: false`, a linked GitHub Release, a reconciled hand-curated CHANGELOG entry, and cross-platform install verification on Linux + Windows + macOS. Epic 5 delivered the four stories that cut the release:
12	
13	| Story | Outcome | Key evidence |
14	|-------|---------|--------------|
15	| 5.1 Pre-v1.0.0 readiness audit | Seven-file envelope expanded to 8 after `update.js` silent-exit-0 bug surfaced during review (sibling-sweep caught a bug the spec's recon missed); 6/10 pre-flight items (H1/H3/H4/H5/H6/H8) fixed in-story; R4/R5/R6 playbook caveats bundled; 4 dispositions recorded without code change (H2/H7/H9/H10); R1/R2/R3/R7/R8 deferred post-v1. 22/22 AC PASS. | `540fdda` audit + `7b84411` footer back-fill + `cd9c711` review patches; audit artifact at `release-audits/v1.0.0-launch-audit.md` (moved out of `docs/` to avoid Starlight dotted-slug mangling) |
16	| 5.2 Cut `v1.0.0-rc.1` + smoke test | **Seven-attempt saga.** The main-dispatch path in `release.yaml` had zero prior E2E coverage (Story 3.2's alpha cut took the `feat/release-workflow` "Skip main push" branch), so the first main-ref dispatch surfaced five distinct pipeline defects. Spawned Stories 3-4 (PR-auto-merge refactor, issue #198) and 3-5 (direct check-runs API poll, issue #202). rc.1 and rc.2 exist as release commits on `main` but were never tagged or published — zero npm/tag footprint. rc.3 PASS on attempt 7. | `3fc1f00` hand-bump + `14f2a69` sign-off + `21afcf6` hygiene patch; issues #198 + #202 both closed 2026-04-23 |
17	| 5.3 Cut `v1.0.0` under `--tag latest` | **First-attempt dispatch success** — 7m 1s wall-clock (well within NFR1's 20-min target). Release env gate approved 18:52:32Z; bot PR #213 admin-bypass-merged 18:56:30Z; `Publish to npm via OIDC trusted publishing` completed at **18:56:39Z** (the NFR6 immutability activation timestamp). CHANGELOG auto-gen block had an empty body (zero `feat:`/`fix:` between rc.3 and v1.0.0 — only `docs(release):` + `chore(release):`) and was reconciled with the hand-curated prose. | Run `24852899833` + `c0b2b67` reconciliation + `4c69f64` review patches |
18	| 5.4 Post-publish + cross-platform smoke | Authored **one new permanent reusable workflow** (`install-smoke.yaml`, ubuntu + windows + macos matrix). Three-leg dispatch cleared 46s wall-clock, all `1.0.0` stdout, all exit 0. **NFR9 60-min window missed** — elapsed `01:27` (27 min past target), classified `missed-target-non-blocking` since no live defect surfaced during the unobserved window. Code review surfaced a HIGH shell-injection defect via `${{ inputs.version }}` — fixed in PR #221 before the retro. Re-dispatch confirmed regression-free. | Run `24856988815` (Commit 1) + run `24858754152` (post-patch) + commits `5b17847` → `d0cddab` → `4e7405a` |
19	
20	**Headline:** v1.0.0 is live, provenanced, cross-platform verified, and cleanly reconciled — on the first attempt for the actual v1.0.0 cut. The RC cycle did its job: five pipeline defects caught on disposable numbers before the critical path.
21	
22	## What went well
23	
24	1. **The RC cycle worked exactly as designed — five pipeline defects caught on disposable numbers.** Story 5.2 burned rc.1 + rc.2 as release commits that never reached npm or a tag. Every defect surfaced (branch-protection `GH013` block on bot-token push to main, `can_approve_pull_request_reviews: false` repo setting, GH partitions check-runs per trigger-event so `gh pr checks --watch` couldn't see workflow_dispatch-originated green checks, aggregated-vs-per-context error messaging, space-preservation in required-context names) was fixed on feature branches + merged before Story 5.3 dispatched. Zero production impact; zero npm dist-tag churn; zero rollback invocations. The `v1.0.0-rc.3` → `v1.0.0` transition ran on the first attempt in 7m 1s because the pipeline had been hardened by the RC saga. This is textbook RC-cycle value.
25	
26	2. **Story 5.3 validated NFR1 with real wall-clock data.** Dispatch-to-complete at 7m 1s (v1.0.0) and 5m 4s (rc.3) — two data points of a rolling-10 window, both well under the 20-min target. Release-env gate wait (2m 45s for v1.0.0) counted inside that envelope. SLSA L2 provenance verified via `npm view bmad-module-skill-forge@1.0.0 --json | jq '.dist.attestations'` — `predicateType: https://slsa.dev/provenance/v1` attached to the tarball. NFR4 100% provenance coverage: MAINTAINED across every release post-OIDC migration (alpha cut, rc.3, v1.0.0).
27	
28	3. **Story 5.1's sibling-sweep caught `update.js` silent-exit-0.** The spec's Dev Notes recon stated "other commands exit 1 on failure" — code review ran a full sweep of `status.js` / `uninstall.js` / `update.js` and found `update.js` had an unconditional `process.exit(0)` at the function tail, mirroring the H1 `install.js` bug. Fix applied; scope envelope expanded 7 → 8 files with explicit disclosure. This is the Epic 4 pattern — "adversarial review on dense prose finds bugs that look like prose" — extended to the sibling-sweep case: recon claims must be verified, not trusted from the spec's prior-art section.
29	
30	4. **Seven-file envelope held on the audit story despite the expansion.** Story 5.1 authored the entire v1.0.0 launch audit, the six gap closures, three R-caveats, and the CHANGELOG hand-curated prose — all in one Commit 1 (`540fdda`) plus the self-referential footer back-fill (`7b84411`). No drive-by edits. The expansion to 8 was explicit + review-driven, not scope creep.
31	
32	5. **Story 5.3's first-attempt dispatch worked because the pipeline was hardened.** Story 3.4 (PR-auto-merge refactor) and Story 3-5 (direct check-runs API poll) both landed before Story 5.3 dispatched. The bot-PR admin-bypass-merge pattern matched rc.3's PR #209; the `Wait for PR approval or admin-bypass merge` step cleared in 0s because merge was already in place by the time the step evaluated. The empty-conventional-commit CHANGELOG block edge case (rc.3 → v1.0.0 had only `docs(release):` + `chore(release):` commits) was expected per `deferred-work.md` 3-3 and reconciled cleanly by AC 10.
33	
34	6. **Story 5.4's `install-smoke.yaml` is a permanent reusable artifact.** Every future release — v1.0.1, v1.1.0, v2.0.0, any RC — can redispatch it with the new version tag and produce identical three-platform evidence with zero additional engineering. `macos-latest` was elected into the matrix on create-story per Dev Notes (A), converting a soft NFR9 manual-spot-check into a reproducible CI artifact. One-time authorship cost, permanent cross-platform floor.
35	
36	7. **Epic 4 carry-forward held across all 14 commitments.** Story 5.1 verified Stories 4.1 + 4.2 merged to main before opening (Commitment 1). Story 5.1 imported H1–H10 + R1–R8 into Dev Notes at create-story time (Commitments 2 + 3). `[DEPRECATED]`-on-stderr divergence audited KEEP (Commitment 4). Task-1 live recon held 15-for-15 across Epics 1–5 (Commitment 5). Adversarial code review with Patch/Defer/Dismiss triage held 15-for-15 (Commitment 6) — now confirmed on audit artifact prose AND workflow code AND install smoke. Downstream-spec narrowing at current-story-review time (Commitment 7) held for Story 5.1 → 5.2 → 5.3 → 5.4 chain. Inline-CLI dry-run at story-authoring time (Commitment 8) — partial: the jq `.dist` shorthand in Story 5.3 AC 11(iii) returned null when run (extraction path wrong), caught post-hoc, not at spec time.
37	
38	8. **Story 5.4's HIGH shell-injection defect was caught before the retro — not shipped to production.** Code review flagged `${{ inputs.version }}` interpolated directly into a `run:` block on `install-smoke.yaml:67`. Both Blind Hunter and Edge Case Hunter flagged it independently (dual independent discovery = high signal). Fix routed the input through `env: VERSION:` with `"$VERSION"` quoted in the script. Semver-regex validation added as companion hardening. Post-patch re-dispatch clean across all three legs. Pattern: **supply-chain-adjacent workflows get two review passes** — first pass caught 3 patches + 4 defers; second pass (on the patch commit itself) caught 2 more medium patches + 5 more low defers. The second pass is load-bearing, not ceremony.
39	
40	## Patterns across all four stories
41	
42	### Primary-code-path-first validation is non-negotiable
43	
44	The five pipeline defects in Story 5.2 ALL lived on the main-dispatch path (`release.yaml § Push commit to main` and downstream). Story 3.2's alpha cut had dispatched from `feat/release-workflow`, which took the `if: github.ref != 'refs/heads/main'` "Skip main push" branch in `release.yaml:239-244` — the main-dispatch code path was therefore completely untested until Story 5.2's first dispatch. **Every workflow-code story whose primary code path is main-dispatch MUST validate via a main-ref dispatch, not a feature-branch dispatch, before being claimed done.** The cost of this discovery was ~6 hours of real time across 7 dispatches, two blocker stories (3-4 + 3-5), and three remediation cycles. The user-memory `feedback_release_pipeline_e2e_validation.md` already captures this. The retro sharpens it into a rule: **feature-branch validation proves the workflow can run; main-ref validation proves the workflow can ship**.
45	
46	### Recon claims must be verified, not trusted
47	
48	Story 5.1's Dev Notes stated "recon verified other commands exit 1" — code review's sibling sweep found `update.js` with `process.exit(0)` at the tail. The recon was wrong. Same class as Epic 4 retro's "do not silently reconcile drift" — the answer is not to trust the spec's prior-art section, but to re-verify recon claims at review time. **Recon = live evidence at Task 1, not spec prose at create-story.**
49	
50	### Adversarial review on dense prose and workflow-generation code keeps finding real bugs
51	
52	Across Epic 5, code review caught:
53	- `update.js` silent-exit-0 (Story 5.1, Blind Hunter sibling sweep)
54	- Verification snippet that breaks on any subsequent `main` commit (Story 5.3 HIGH patch: `[ "$(git rev-parse 'v1.0.0^{}')" = "$(git log main -1 --format='%H')" ]` — currently false whenever main advances; replaced with `git merge-base --is-ancestor v1.0.0 main`)
55	- Step-27 prose inversion in the launch audit (`github.ref == 'refs/heads/main' evaluated true, so the non-main branch is correctly bypassed` — non-sequitur; rewritten)
56	- HIGH shell-injection via `${{ inputs.version }}` in `install-smoke.yaml` (Story 5.4, dual independent discovery)
57	- Semver-regex validation missing (Story 5.4, edge reviewer)
58	- Matrix-line-number evidence stale post-R4/R5/R6 inserts (Story 5.1, Edge Case Hunter)
59	- NFR1 dispatch timestamp off by 1s vs GitHub API (Story 5.3, edge reviewer)
60	
61	**Pattern holds 15-for-15 across Epics 1–5.** Confirmed load-bearing on (a) workflow YAML, (b) operational documentation prose, (c) audit artifact prose, (d) CLI help output — every deliverable surface that this project has shipped benefits from the Patch/Defer/Dismiss triage.
62	
63	### Second-pass review on the hygiene patch surfaces more defects
64	
65	Story 5.2's hygiene patch `21afcf6` received a Round 2 review. Round 2 flagged four additional patches (derogation para missing PR links, attempts-table self-referential causation, skill-count weasel-word, install transcript exit-code claim mismatched to fenced block). Story 5.4's patch commit `d0cddab` received a second pass that surfaced two more medium patches (`workflow_dispatch` semantic allowlist, identity-validation of `OUTPUT` vs requested `VERSION`) and 5 more low defers. **When the deliverable is supply-chain-adjacent (release pipeline, install verifier, npm-publish path), budget a second review pass on the hygiene commit itself.** The first pass audits the code; the second pass audits the fix.
66	
67	### Scope envelope adherence holds when pre-execution ban-list is explicit
68	
69	All four Epic 5 stories named their scope envelope in Dev Notes / Project Structure Notes BEFORE execution:
70	- Story 5.1: 7-file envelope named; expanded to 8 with explicit review-driven disclosure when `update.js` surfaced
71	- Story 5.2: `release-audits/` + `package.json` + `package-lock.json` named; no CHANGELOG or RELEASING.md edits (those belonged to Story 5.3)
72	- Story 5.3: 3-file envelope named; `v1.0.0-rc.1` → `v1.0.0 under --tag latest` section rename held in the same CHANGELOG+RELEASING.md+audit envelope
73	- Story 5.4: 3-file envelope named; zero repo-source code changes; only workflow + audit + RELEASING.md cross-reference
74	
75	Pattern carried forward **15-for-15 across Epics 1–5.** Pre-execution naming is more effective than post-execution grep sweeps.
76	
77	### AC authoring drift across four categories
78	
79	Four distinct spec-authoring defects surfaced during Epic 5:
80	
81	1. **Topology claims go stale when the workflow changes.** Story 5.2 AC 8(a) said "main advances by exactly one commit" — wrote for the pre-Story-3.4 single-commit path; Story 3.4's PR-auto-merge refactor made main advance by two commits (merge + release). Disclosed in Story 5.2 preamble + audit artifact, not formally amended.
82	2. **Foreclosure clauses collide with latent infeasibility.** Story 5.2 AC 9(d) wrote "If prompts don't accept empty-lines-as-defaults, that IS a defect requiring a new RC per AC 12" — the install UI's `required: true` multiselect cannot be driven by piped stdin (no way for AC 9(d) to pass by the literal clause it wrote). Reclassified as documentation defect via issue #211; honest label is "spec deviation, foreclosure overridden by pragmatic judgment."
83	3. **"Spirit" framing is a lexical stretch.** Story 5.2's audit used AC 12's "spirit" to cover pipeline-defect RC rollover (rc.1/rc.2 failures were all upstream of `npm publish`; AC 12's letter scopes to RC-content defects + npm immutability). The hygiene patch added strong negative-evidence (`git tag -l` + `npm view versions --json` both empty) that closes AC 12's actual contract.
84	4. **Char-count arithmetic errors.** Twice: Story 5.1 AC 21 (spec claimed 69, actual 53) and Story 5.3 AC 15 (spec claimed 64, actual 78). Implementers used the verbatim string both times; zero functional divergence.
85	
86	**Decision (per retro framing):** fold corrections into next-touch, not a standalone retroactive amendment pass. The four categories are archaeological on dead ACs; the meta-lesson goes into Commitment 13 below. Specifically:
87	- AC 8(a) topology → resolves when Epic 6 next touches the audit
88	- AC 9(d) foreclosure → closes with issue #211 (which says the same thing)
89	- AC 12 "spirit" → revisit next time a release-pipeline story lands; the contract AC 12 actually cared about (npm immutability) was satisfied
90	- AC 15 char-count → dismiss; verbatim-string use meant zero implementation divergence
91	
92	## Load-bearing inputs carried into Epic 6
93	
94	Epic 6 is cleanup: Story 6-1 delete `publish.yaml` + `manual-release.yaml`; Story 6-2 remove `release:*` scripts from `package.json`; Story 6-3 remove `NPM_TOKEN` secret from repo settings. Epic 5 outputs feed Epic 6 directly.
95	
96	### Epic 6 pre-flight items (inherited from Epic 5 reviews)
97	
98	**(P1) — `rc` dist-tag still pinned at `1.0.0-rc.3` post-GA.** Source: Story 5.4 deferred (S54-D1). Users installing via `npm install bmad-module-skill-forge@rc` remain stuck on the release candidate. NFR6 forbids mutating the npm state mid-story; Epic 6 (or a standalone ops ticket) owns the decision. Options: (a) `npm dist-tag rm bmad-module-skill-forge rc`; (b) `npm dist-tag add bmad-module-skill-forge@1.0.0 rc` (pin rc at GA); (c) leave as-is with a note in RELEASING.md explaining the artifact. **Recommendation:** (a) remove — aligns with "post-v1.0.0 the RC channel is closed; use `@latest`." Enshrine in `docs/RELEASING.md` so future cut-ops don't recreate the pattern.
99	
100	**(P2) — GH Actions default 90-day log retention rots audit evidence.** Source: Story 5.4 deferred (S54-D2). `release-audits/v1.0.0-launch-audit.md` references run URLs (`24852899833`, `24856988815`, `24858754152`) as primary evidence. When GitHub prunes those runs (default 90d), the links die and the matrix-result tables become unverifiable. Cross-cuts every audit artifact that references a run URL. Mitigations: (a) upload summary artifact from `install-smoke.yaml` with structured JSON of per-leg stdout/exit/timings + retain for 400d; (b) copy raw per-leg stdout into the audit artifact verbatim (eliminates GH retention dependence); (c) increase repo-level log retention. **Recommendation:** (b) as a minimum — audit artifacts are the canonical record, so they should be self-contained rather than linking to soon-to-rot run URLs. (a) as a bonus for future releases. Address in Epic 6 or a standalone "audit-evidence hardening" story.
101	
102	**(P3) — NFR9 needs automation, not policy softening.** Source: Story 5.4 `missed-target-non-blocking` classification. The 60-min window is effectively unachievable under solo-maintainer operation because it requires a maintainer-in-the-loop for manual `install-smoke.yaml` dispatch. **Recommendation:** add `on: workflow_run: { workflows: [Release], types: [completed] }` OR `on: release: { types: [published] }` trigger to `install-smoke.yaml` so it auto-dispatches in the same few minutes as `release.yaml` completion. One-time workflow edit; permanent NFR9 enforcement. Track as a post-v1.0.0 release-pipeline hardening story (new) — candidate for Epic 6 scope expansion or a dedicated follow-up.
103	
104	**(P4) — Orphaned `feat/release-workflow` branch + `v0.10.1-alpha.0` tag.** Source: Epic 3 retro standing flag #15. The branch carries the orphaned commit `2a57dcbd` reachable only via tag `v0.10.1-alpha.0` + branch `feat/release-workflow`. Do NOT delete the branch until GitHub retention behavior on orphaned commits is confirmed. Story 6-adjacent cleanup story is the natural home. **Recommendation:** verify reachability post-branch-deletion on a throwaway test case first (e.g. delete a test branch with an orphaned commit, confirm the commit survives via tag reference for 90+ days), then remove `feat/release-workflow`.
105	
106	### Remaining standing flags (no fire date)
107	
108	**(P5) — `prevent_self_review: false → true` on the `release` environment + revoke solo-maintainer ruleset bypass.** Source: Epic 1 standing flag, clarified by Epic 3 Story 3.3 AC #6 drift. Both flips needed together when a second reviewer onboards. Fires when the project grows past solo maintenance.
109	
110	**(P6) — `install.js:23` silent-exit-0 fix pattern.** Source: Epic 2 retro #9, landed in Story 5.1 for `install.js` + extended to `update.js` via review. Remaining exit-path audit surface: new CLI commands MUST follow the "explicit exit per branch" pattern; add an ESLint rule or tools/validate-* check if this recurs.

STABILITY.md Path B handles the semantics (no programmatic API); the field itself is still a minor pre-v1.0.0 cleanup candidate that slipped past Epic 5. Addressable in Epic 6 scope if `package.json` is being touched for Story 6-2 anyway.

Removing them — Story 6-1's literal scope — also removes that attack surface. Cite S54's shell-injection catch as additional rationale in the PR description.

Story 6-2 deletes them entirely. Issue #211 (piped-stdin install infeasibility) is tangentially related — it tracks a separate install-UX defect. Do NOT bundle #211 into Story 6-2. They're separate tickets in separate territories.

Cite Story 5.3's run URL `24852899833` + provenance URL as the "OIDC has shipped v1.0.0" evidence.

Landing them separately keeps the rollback surface small; a post-6-1 regression doesn't require reverting 6-2/6-3 state. Previous epics have shown that separate PRs = separate reverts = lower blast radius.

144	### Process improvements (continue across Epic 6+)
145	
146	6. **Primary-code-path-first validation for workflow-code stories.** Any workflow-code story whose primary code path is main-dispatch MUST validate via a main-ref dispatch, not a feature-branch dispatch, before being claimed done. Derived from Epic 5 Story 5.2's 7-attempt saga. Feature-branch validation proves the workflow *can run*; main-ref validation proves the workflow *can ship*. For Epic 6: Story 6-1/6-2/6-3 are file-deletion stories, not workflow-code stories — this rule doesn't apply directly. Carries to any future `release.yaml` / `install-smoke.yaml` / `quality.yaml` edits.

The spec's Dev Notes said "recon verified others exit 1" and it was wrong. Code reviewers should re-run recon CLI on prior-art claims during adversarial review — not just against the diff, but against the state the diff assumes.

153	
154	10. **Supply-chain-adjacent workflows get two review passes.** First pass audits the code; second pass audits the fix. Confirmed load-bearing on Story 5.2 (Round 2 on hygiene patch surfaced four more patches) and Story 5.4 (second pass on patch commit surfaced two medium + five low). When the deliverable is release-pipeline or install-verification code, budget the second pass. New for Epic 6.
155	
156	11. **When a downstream story's premise changes mid-epic, patch the downstream planning artifact in the current story's review.** 5-for-5 across Epics 2/3/4/5. Unchanged.
157	
158	12. **ACs that contain inline scripts or operational CLI MUST be dry-run at story-authoring time** (Epic 3 retro #8). Holds 5-for-5, with the AC 11(iii) `jq '.dist' {version, ...}` shorthand catch in Story 5.3 as the worked reminder — the spec's shorthand returned null when executed (attempted top-level extraction of nested keys). Unchanged.
159	
160	13. **NEW: AC authoring has four recurring failure modes — (a) topology claims go stale when underlying workflows change; (b) foreclosure clauses collide with latent infeasibility; (c) "spirit" framing is a lexical stretch; (d) char-count arithmetic.** Apply to future spec authoring: (a) re-check topology claims against current workflow state at create-story; (b) never write "THAT IS A DEFECT requiring a new RC" without verifying the preconditions are actually achievable; (c) if the AC's letter doesn't cover a scenario, write a new clause rather than stretching "spirit"; (d) run `echo -n "..." | wc -c` on any verbatim char-count claim. Archived ACs stay as they are — the lesson goes to future stories. See Epic 4 retro #8 for the parent pattern.

That worked: Story 5.2 rc.3 PASS + Story 5.3 v1.0.0 first-attempt-success effectively proved Story 3-5's Wait-step refactor. **Pattern to keep:** when a workflow-code story's validation is downstream, name the downstream story + the downstream event + the close-out transition explicitly in the story's Completion Notes List. Don't leave the transition floating.

Retro is the natural forum: it reviews the whole epic lineage, not just the current story. For Epic 6 retro: sweep sprint-status for stale entries at retro time; flip anything whose done-condition has been met.

1	# Story 6.1: Delete legacy `publish.yaml` and `manual-release.yaml` workflows
2	
3	Status: done
4	
5	<!-- Note: Validation is optional. Run validate-create-story for quality check before dev-story. -->
6	
7	> **Precondition summary (2026-04-24 create-story time):** Epic 5 is `done`. v1.0.0 is live on npm under `--tag latest` with SLSA L2 provenance (`npm view bmad-module-skill-forge@latest version` → `1.0.0`). `main` tip is [`4e7405a`](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/commit/4e7405a) — PR #221 merge (Story 5.4 code-review patches). `release-audits/v1.0.0-launch-audit.md` carries both `### Decision: v1.0.0 launch status = LAUNCHED` (`:648`) and `### Decision: v1.0.0 cross-platform verification status = VERIFIED` (`:720`); the top-level `## Sign-off` is in four-sentence form closing Epic 5. `.github/workflows/install-smoke.yaml` is live on `main` and has one green three-leg dispatch on record (run `24858754152`, post-patch). `sprint-status.yaml` shows `epic-5: done`, `5-4: done`, `epic-6: backlog` (flipping to `in-progress` on this create-story call per the first-story-in-epic rule).
8	>
9	> **What Story 6.1 is:** a pure workflow-deletion cleanup under FR19. The two legacy release paths (`publish.yaml` + `manual-release.yaml`) have been inert since Story 3.3 Patch A (tag-push trigger neutralization) and were marked DEPRECATED in their top-of-file headers by Story 4.2. v1.0.0 shipped via `release.yaml` (OIDC trusted publishing), closing the precondition clause in the Epic 6 scope statement: "once the canonical OIDC path is proven by the shipped v1.0.0." Story 6.1 now removes the inert files and prunes the documentation references that assume they still exist. No release is cut; no `release.yaml` / `install-smoke.yaml` edit; no npm state change; no secret/environment change.
10	>
11	> **Relationship to Epic 6 siblings and upstream work:** Story 6-1 ships first (this story). Story 6-2 (remove `release:*` scripts from `package.json`) follows, Story 6-3 (remove `NPM_TOKEN` repo secret) last. **No bundling** per Epic 5 retrospective Commitment 5 — distinct risk profiles (workflow deletion / script deletion / secret deletion) mean separate PRs = separate reverts = smaller rollback surface. Commitment 2 additionally notes: removing `publish.yaml` + `manual-release.yaml` also closes a HIGH shell-injection concern surface — `manual-release.yaml:59-61` interpolates `${{ github.event.inputs.version_bump }}` directly into bash `case` blocks without the `env:`-isolation pattern that Story 5.4 introduced for `install-smoke.yaml`. The deletion is the fix.
12	
13	## Story
14	
15	As the maintainer,
16	I want the deprecated legacy workflows (`publish.yaml` + `manual-release.yaml`) physically removed from `.github/workflows/`,
17	so that the repo's release surface is single-source-of-truth (`release.yaml`), no operator (human or bot) can accidentally invoke an inert path, and the HIGH shell-injection attack surface carried by `manual-release.yaml`'s unshielded `${{ github.event.inputs.* }}` interpolations is eliminated in one stroke.
18	
19	## Acceptance Criteria
20	
21	1. **Scope envelope — one commit, four files touched.** Subject: `chore(release): delete legacy publish.yaml and manual-release.yaml workflows (Story 6.1)`. Touches EXACTLY four files:
22	   - `.github/workflows/publish.yaml` (DELETED — the file is physically removed from `.github/workflows/`)
23	   - `.github/workflows/manual-release.yaml` (DELETED — physically removed)
24	   - `CONTRIBUTING.md` (MODIFIED — prune the `Releasing` bullet referencing both legacy workflows as "do not invoke directly"; AC 6)
25	   - `docs/RELEASING.md` (MODIFIED — prune the Scenario G emergency-hatch subsection + Scenario G Prevention `id-token: write` expected-set + the `NPM_TOKEN § legacy residual` paragraph to no longer claim `publish.yaml` still uses the token; AC 7)
26	
27	   **Verification:** `git show --stat HEAD` on the merge commit returns the four-file set with `D` for the two workflows and `M` for the two docs; `git log main --grep='Story 6.1' --oneline` returns exactly the commit subject above.
28	
29	2. **Epic 5 close-out precondition verification — required before branch open.** Per Epic 5 retrospective Commitment 1, the dev agent SHALL verify on `origin/main` at Task 1 time that every one of the following is true, and record the recon transcript verbatim in Dev Agent Record § Debug Log References. If any precondition fails, HALT — Story 6.1 cannot ship without Epic 5 truly closed:
30	   - (a) `grep -c '^### Decision: v1.0.0 launch status$' release-audits/v1.0.0-launch-audit.md` → `1`; the following line SHALL contain `LAUNCHED`
31	   - (b) `grep -c '^### Decision: v1.0.0 cross-platform verification status$' release-audits/v1.0.0-launch-audit.md` → `1`; the following line SHALL contain `VERIFIED`
32	   - (c) `test -f .github/workflows/install-smoke.yaml && echo OK` → `OK`
33	   - (d) `grep -c '^### Cutting v1.0.0 under --tag latest$' docs/RELEASING.md` → `1`
34	   - (e) `grep -E '^(epic-5|5-4-post-publish-verification)' _bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/sprint-status.yaml` → both lines end in `done`
35	   - (f) `gh api repos/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/contents/.github/workflows/publish.yaml --jq '.name'` → `publish.yaml` (confirms the file is actually on remote `main` to be deleted)
36	   - (g) `gh api repos/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/contents/.github/workflows/manual-release.yaml --jq '.name'` → `manual-release.yaml` (ditto)
37	
38	3. **PR description requirements.** The PR body SHALL include verbatim:
39	   - (a) An explicit reference to FR19 — e.g., `Closes FR19 "delete publish.yaml/manual-release.yaml; remove release:* (Story 6.2); remove NPM_TOKEN secret (Story 6.3)"`. Story 6.1 satisfies the first clause only.
40	   - (b) The Epic 5 close-out evidence: Story 5.3 run URL `<https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/actions/runs/24852899833>` + Story 5.4 post-patch run URL `<https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/actions/runs/24858754152>` as the "enabling precondition" citation (the OIDC path shipped v1.0.0 cleanly, so the legacy paths are provably unneeded).
41	   - (c) The shell-injection-surface rationale: `manual-release.yaml:59-61` interpolates `${{ github.event.inputs.version_bump }}` directly into a bash `case` without the `env:`-isolation pattern Story 5.4 introduced for `install-smoke.yaml` (PR #221). Deletion closes this HIGH vector — per Epic 5 retrospective Commitment 2.
42	   - (d) A four-file touched-list with one-line per-file purpose (matching AC 1 D/M breakdown).
43	   - (e) NO `Fixes #NN` trailer (there is no same-repo issue tracking this story; FR19 reference form is sufficient). NO `_bmad-output/todo/` references (per user-memory `feedback_no_issue_numbers_in_pr.md`). Cite Story ACs in `AC N` form (not `#N`).
44

156	## Dev Notes
157	
158	### What this story actually is
159	
160	A **pure workflow-deletion cleanup** covering one clause of FR19. Delete `.github/workflows/publish.yaml` + `.github/workflows/manual-release.yaml`, and prune the three stale prose references in `CONTRIBUTING.md` + `docs/RELEASING.md` that assume those files still exist. Single commit, single PR, four files touched. The deletion is safe NOW because v1.0.0 shipped via `release.yaml` OIDC (Story 5.3 run `24852899833`) and cross-platform install was verified via `install-smoke.yaml` (Story 5.4 run `24858754152`, post-patch `24858754152`) — the OIDC path is proven load-bearing, so the legacy paths are provably redundant.
161	
162	**What this story is NOT:**
163	
164	- NOT a release. Zero `release.yaml` dispatch, zero `install-smoke.yaml` dispatch, zero npm state change.
165	- NOT a `release:*` script removal. Story 6-2 handles that. The `release:*` stubs keep their Story 4.2 `[DEPRECATED]`-on-stderr + `exit 1` wrapper until then.
166	- NOT an `NPM_TOKEN` secret removal. Story 6-3 handles that. The secret stays at repo scope through Story 6-1 + 6-2.
167	- NOT a `docs/RELEASING.md` rewrite. Surgical pruning only — the three AC-7 edit points are the full scope; the rest of the 700+ line document is untouched.
168	- NOT a `release.yaml` header-comment edit. `release.yaml:4` says `Consolidates publish.yaml + manual-release.yaml` — that is past-tense historical framing ("consolidates" in the present-perfect sense of "is the consolidation of") and stays. If the dev agent wants to pedantically tense-shift this to `Consolidated publish.yaml + manual-release.yaml (deleted in Story 6.1)` the edit is welcome but OPTIONAL; it does not count against AC 1's four-file list if skipped.
169	- NOT an audit-artifact append. `release-audits/v1.0.0-launch-audit.md` stays at its Story-5.4 close-out state. Epic 6 as a whole may append a `## Epic 6 Cleanup` block when Story 6-3 closes, but that is NOT Story 6-1's scope.
170	- NOT a second-pass review story. These are file deletions — the first-pass code review is sufficient (Epic 5 retrospective Commitment 10's "two review passes" trigger is supply-chain-adjacent workflows, not workflow-deletion commits).
171	
172	### Load-bearing decisions already made at create-story (no Dev-Agent re-derivation needed)
173	
174	**(A) Single-commit envelope, not two.** Story 5.4 used two commits because it needed to dispatch a workflow between them and capture ground-truth evidence. Story 6.1 has no workflow to dispatch — deletion is atomic and verifiable by post-merge `curl -sI` + `gh workflow list`. One commit keeps the rollback surface a single PR revert.
175	
176	**(B) Four files in one commit, not four commits.** Splitting deletions-from-prose-edits would land `main` in a transient state where `publish.yaml` is gone but `docs/RELEASING.md` still documents its emergency hatch — a documented trap for any operator reading the playbook during the window between merges. One commit means `main` never carries that inconsistency.
177	
178	**(C) `CONTRIBUTING.md` bullet is deleted, not rewritten.** The bullet's sole purpose is to warn against invoking the legacy workflows; once they're gone, the warning is vacuous. Adding a replacement bullet like "these workflows have been removed" would be CHANGELOG-style narration that belongs in git history, not in a maintainer onboarding doc. The Story 4.2 precedent (which authored the bullet) is inverted cleanly — the deprecation warning is retired when the deprecated thing is retired.
179	
180	**(D) Scenario G emergency hatch is deleted, not reworded.** The hatch was a real operational path predicated on `publish.yaml` being dispatchable; that premise dies with the file. Keeping a rewritten "there is no emergency hatch" paragraph would be LLM-Optimization noise — better to delete the sub-bullet and let the reader infer from Scenario G's remaining Case 1 + Case 2 that PR-revert is the only path. A single short replacement sub-bullet noting "recovery is PR-revert per Case 2" anchors the inference without verbosity.
181	
182	**(E) `docs/RELEASING.md:431` historical reference stays.** The line says `Story 3.3 Patch A neutralized the legacy publish.yaml v* tag trigger` — past-tense historical context. Deleting it would erase the audit lineage that explains WHY `release.yaml` is single-rooted today. Story 6.1's deletion of the file doesn't invalidate the history of what happened to the file when it was alive. Keep.
183	
184	**(F) Shell-injection surface closure is a free benefit, not the primary justification.** AC 3(c) cites the `manual-release.yaml:59-61` HIGH vector as rationale-adjacent, not AC-blocking. The story's primary AC is FR19 (delete the files post-v1.0.0). The shell-injection closure is Epic 5 retrospective Commitment 2's "additional rationale" — amplifies the value proposition for the PR reviewer, doesn't drive the scope decision. If the legacy workflows were spotlessly secure, this story would still ship; the shell-injection vector just makes the ship more obviously correct.
185	
186	**(G) `gh workflow list` propagation lag is cosmetic, not blocking.** Epic 4 Story 4.1 retrospective D2 captured that GitHub keeps `state:"active"` for a period after file deletion (observed on `env-gate-test.yaml`). If AC 10(b) or 10(c) shows residual active-state at first poll, re-poll after ~60s and record both observations — this is expected GH Actions API behavior and does NOT block Story 6.1 close. The load-bearing verification is AC 11's `gh workflow run ... → not found`, which exercises the actual "can this be dispatched" question rather than the cached listing.
187	
188	**(H) Branch name `feat/v1-cleanup-legacy-workflows` follows Story 5.3/5.4 branch naming convention.** Prefix `feat/v1-` keeps the v1-era cleanup branches grouped together; `cleanup-legacy-workflows` is specific enough to distinguish from Story 6-2's forthcoming `feat/v1-cleanup-release-scripts` and Story 6-3's `feat/v1-cleanup-npm-token`. Do NOT reuse any Epic 5 branch (`feat/v1-install-smoke-workflow`, `feat/v1-post-publish-verify`, `feat/v1-install-smoke-review-patches`, etc.). Fresh branch.
189	
190	### Coupling with past, current, and future stories
191	
192	**Past (enables / blocks this story):**
193	
194	- **Story 5.3 shipped v1.0.0 via OIDC at `2026-04-23T18:56:39Z`** — satisfies Epic 6 scope statement's "once the canonical OIDC path is proven by the shipped v1.0.0." Precondition for deletion.
195	- **Story 5.4 ran cross-platform install smoke via `install-smoke.yaml`** — three-leg matrix clean (ubuntu + windows + macos); confirmed no platform-specific regression. Satisfies the "v1.0.0 actually works" precondition empirically, not just by-virtue-of-shipping.
196	- **Epic 5 retrospective (2026-04-24) set Commitments 1, 2, 5** — live at create-story. Commitment 1 → AC 2 precondition set; Commitment 2 → AC 3(c) shell-injection rationale; Commitment 5 → no bundling with 6-2/6-3.
197	- **Story 3.3 Patch A neutralized `publish.yaml`'s `v*` tag-push trigger** — `publish.yaml` has been inert since that patch (no automatic fire on tag push). Only `workflow_dispatch:` remains, which the Scenario G emergency hatch was the last active consumer of. This story is closing that hatch.
198	- **Story 4.2 added `DEPRECATED` headers** to both legacy workflow files (lines 1 of each). Precedent for the "retire the deprecation warning when the thing is retired" principle in Dev Notes (C).
199	- **Story 1.1 branch protection on `main`** — AC 9 PR-only constraint.
200	
201	**Current (this story):**
202	
203	- Four files touched: 2 workflow deletions + 2 doc prunes. One commit, one PR, one merge.
204	- No workflow dispatch. No npm state change. No secret change.
205	- Epic 6 flips `backlog → in-progress` at create-story time (first-story-in-epic rule). Stays `in-progress` until 6-3 closes.
206	- 6-1 walks `backlog → ready-for-dev → in-progress → review → done`.
207	
208	**Future (this story feeds):**
209	
210	- **Story 6-2** (remove `release:*` scripts from `package.json`) — unblocked by Story 6-1 close. The `release:*` stubs remain deprecated-with-exit-1 through that story.
211	- **Story 6-3** (remove `NPM_TOKEN` secret) — unblocked by Story 6-1 close AND Story 6-2 close. Requires that no workflow on `main` reads `secrets.NPM_TOKEN` — Story 6-1 removes the two workflows that did; `release.yaml` uses OIDC, not the token.
212	- **Epic 6 retrospective** — sweeps sprint-status for stale entries per Epic 5 retro Commitment 15, reviews the three-cleanup-story arc's rollback posture, captures any emergent post-v1.0.0 patterns.
213	- **Next release cut (v1.0.1 / v1.1.0)** — inherits a single-rooted release surface with one release workflow + one install-smoke workflow. No operator confusion about which workflow to dispatch.
214	- **Any future Scenario G incident** — the playbook's recovery is PR-revert only; no emergency hatch exists. This is a narrower recovery surface but a cleaner one (everything flows through branch protection + code review, matching NFR2 + NFR3).

1	# Story 6.2: Remove `release:*` scripts from `package.json`
2	
3	Status: done
4	
5	<!-- Note: Validation is optional. Run validate-create-story for quality check before dev-story. -->
6	
7	> **Precondition summary (2026-04-24 create-story time):** Epic 5 is `done` (v1.0.0 live on npm under `--tag latest` with SLSA L2 provenance since `2026-04-23T18:56:39Z`). Story 6.1 is `done` — PR #222 merged at commit [`309f558`](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/commit/309f558) on `2026-04-24T12:55:35Z`; `.github/workflows/publish.yaml` and `.github/workflows/manual-release.yaml` are physically gone from `main` (verified via `curl -sI` returning `HTTP/2 404` and `gh workflow list` omitting `Publish to NPM` / `Manual Release`). `main` tip is `309f558` — the legacy-workflow cleanup commit. Sprint-status shows `epic-6: in-progress`, `6-1-…: done`, `6-2-…: backlog → ready-for-dev` (flipping on this create-story call), `6-3-…: backlog`, `epic-6-retrospective: optional`.
8	>
9	> **What Story 6.2 is:** a surgical `package.json` cleanup under FR19 (clause 2 of 3). The five `release:*`-shape script keys in `package.json:48-52` (`release`, `release:major`, `release:minor`, `release:patch`, `release:prerelease`) have been inert `[DEPRECATED]` fail-loud stubs since Story 4.2 (`echo >&2 && exit 1` prefix). With Story 6.1 having removed the legacy workflows they were warning about, the warning bullet in `CONTRIBUTING.md:85` and the stub scripts themselves are now stale-by-construction and can be removed outright. Two files touched in one commit: the `package.json` script removals and the `CONTRIBUTING.md` bullet prune.
10	>
11	> **Relationship to Epic 6 siblings.** Story 6.1 shipped first (workflow deletion, done). Story 6.2 ships this PR (script deletion). Story 6.3 (remove `NPM_TOKEN` repo secret) follows. **No bundling** per Epic 5 retrospective Commitment 5 — distinct risk profiles (workflow deletion / script deletion / secret deletion) mean separate PRs = separate reverts = smaller rollback surface. Epic 5 retro Commitment 3 additionally warns: Story 6.2 MUST NOT bundle issue #211 (piped-stdin install infeasibility) — that is a separate install-UX defect in separate territory.
12	
13	## Story
14	
15	As the maintainer,
16	I want the five deprecated `release:*` script keys physically removed from `package.json` and the now-stale warning bullet pruned from `CONTRIBUTING.md`,
17	so that the npm script surface reflects the actual supported operations, no operator is tempted to bypass the canonical `.github/workflows/release.yaml` CI path by invoking a local `npm run release:patch`, and the repo's release-retirement arc (Story 6.1 deleted the legacy workflows; this story deletes the legacy npm script entrypoints) advances one more step toward the single-rooted OIDC-only surface Story 6.3 will close.
18

168	## Dev Notes
169	
170	### What this story actually is
171	
172	A **surgical two-file cleanup** covering one clause of FR19. Delete the five `release:*`-shape script keys from `package.json` (`release`, `release:major`, `release:minor`, `release:patch`, `release:prerelease`) and prune the now-stale `Do not use npm run release:*` bullet at `CONTRIBUTING.md:85`. Single commit, single PR, two files touched. The deletion is safe NOW because Story 6.1 (PR #222, merged `309f558` on `2026-04-24T12:55:35Z`) removed the legacy workflows the stubs were warning against — the deprecation message ("Use `.github/workflows/release.yaml`") remains valid (that's still the canonical path), but the muscle-memory-catch value proposition evaporates when the legacy targets are gone. The stubs now warn against a non-existent alternative; the bullet says a cleanup pass "removes them outright"; the logical closure of both is deletion.
173	
174	**What this story is NOT:**
175	
176	- NOT a release. Zero `release.yaml` dispatch, zero `install-smoke.yaml` dispatch, zero npm state change. NFR6 v1.0.0 immutability is vacuously respected (the registry's published v1.0.0 `package.json` is byte-frozen on npm and cannot be affected by repo edits).
177	- NOT a `docs/RELEASING.md` edit. Pre-edit recon (AC 2(g)) confirms `docs/RELEASING.md` contains zero references to `release:*` scripts — Story 4.1's rollback playbook never documented them as a recovery path (it points only to `release.yaml` GitHub Actions dispatch). Epic 6 acceptance criterion (c) ("`docs/RELEASING.md` SHALL remain the canonical reference and SHALL describe only the `release.yaml`-via-GitHub-Actions path") is satisfied by Story 4.1 + current state, not by this story's edits — it's a verification condition, not an edit directive.
178	- NOT a README.md edit. Recon confirms README has no `release:*` references either.
179	- NOT a `release-audits/v1.0.0-launch-audit.md` edit. The `:123` transcript of the `release:patch` stub body is historical audit evidence (Story 4.2 `[DEPRECATED]`-on-stderr divergence record) — same preservation principle as Story 6.1 AC 7(d) preserving `docs/RELEASING.md:431`'s Story 3.3 Patch A historical reference. Do NOT prune.
180	- NOT an `NPM_TOKEN` secret removal. Story 6-3's territory. The secret stays untouched through this story.
181	- NOT a `package.json.main` field change. Epic 5 retro P7 soft-suggested this as "addressable if `package.json` is being touched for 6-2 anyway"; Epic 5 retro Commitment 5's no-bundling discipline overrides that soft suggestion here (distinct risk profiles = distinct reverts). Declined; tracked as standing flag for a future story.
182	- NOT an issue #211 fix. Epic 5 retro Commitment 3 explicitly forbids bundling. The piped-stdin install infeasibility is a CLI-UX defect in separate territory.
183	
184	### Load-bearing decisions already made at create-story (no Dev-Agent re-derivation needed)
185	
186	**(A) Single-commit envelope, not two.** The five-scripts + one-bullet edit is atomic — splitting the script deletion from the bullet prune would land `main` in a transient state where the scripts are gone but `CONTRIBUTING.md` still warns against them (reader confusion). One commit, one PR, one revert surface. Same reasoning as Story 6.1 Dev Notes (A).
187	
188	**(B) Five keys, not "release:alpha/beta + whatever else the epic mentioned".** The epic's AC enumeration (`e.g., release:patch, release:minor, release:major, release:alpha, release:beta`) is non-exhaustive — the `e.g.` admits the list isn't canonical. The authoritative set is whatever exists in `package.json` at Task 1 time. Pre-edit recon (AC 2(e)) confirms five: `release`, `release:major`, `release:minor`, `release:patch`, `release:prerelease`. `release:alpha` and `release:beta` do NOT exist in this repo (nor did they per `git log --all -- package.json | grep -i 'release:alpha\|release:beta'` returning nothing). The spec defensively widens the recon check to catch any unknown `release:*` that may have been added post-retro (zero probability, but the check costs nothing).
189	
190	**(C) `CONTRIBUTING.md` bullet is deleted, not rewritten.** The bullet's sole purpose is to warn against muscle-memory invocation of soon-to-be-removed stubs. Once the stubs ARE removed, the warning is vacuous — invoking a nonexistent script produces an unambiguous `Missing script` error, which is a louder warning than any prose bullet. Adding a replacement bullet like "these scripts have been removed; use `.github/workflows/release.yaml`" would be CHANGELOG-style narration that belongs in git history, not in a maintainer onboarding doc. Same principle as Story 6.1 Dev Notes (C): retire the deprecation warning when the deprecated thing is retired.
191	
192	**(D) No replacement bullet even if the `## Releasing` section looks thin post-edit.** Post-edit, the section has: (1) canonical-path bullet pointing at `release.yaml` + GitHub Actions, (2) `See docs/RELEASING.md` paragraph. That is sufficient for a contributor's onboarding need — the two bullets say "use this" + "read the full procedure here." Adding a third bullet about "scripts removed" would be retroactive narration; git log is the audit log, not `CONTRIBUTING.md`.
193	
194	**(E) `release-audits/v1.0.0-launch-audit.md:123` historical reference stays.** The transcript shows the Story 4.2 stub body with `[DEPRECATED]`-on-stderr routing — it is evidence that the audit at v1.0.0 launch time recorded a conscious divergence decision. Deleting it would erase the audit lineage. The same principle applies as Story 6.1 AC 7(d) for `docs/RELEASING.md:431`. Audit artifacts are point-in-time records; they don't follow repo state forward.
195	
196	**(F) `package.json.main` field stays untouched even though the file is being touched.** Epic 5 retro P7 argued "addressable if package.json is being touched for 6-2 anyway" — this seems seductive but the costs are: (1) a `main`-field change couples the script-removal revert surface with an API-surface decision surface (two distinct operational concerns, per Commitment 5 the exact anti-pattern Epic 5 retro flagged); (2) P7's own framing marks it a "standing flag," which is retro-housekeeping territory, not current-story territory; (3) STABILITY.md Path B already handles the semantic concern by declaring no programmatic API. Decline here; if the user wants to bundle it, they can elect that at code-review time — the rejected-by-default stance keeps the commit's scope claim honest.
197	
198	**(G) Branch name `feat/v1-cleanup-release-scripts` follows Story 5.3/5.4/6.1 convention.** Prefix `feat/v1-` keeps the v1-era cleanup branches grouped; `cleanup-release-scripts` is specific enough to distinguish from Story 6-1's `feat/v1-cleanup-legacy-workflows` and the forthcoming Story 6-3's `feat/v1-cleanup-npm-token`. Do NOT reuse any Epic 5 branch or Story 6-1's branch.
199	
200	**(H) Lockfile invariance is load-bearing, not merely expected.** Epic 5 retro's Story 2.1 post-mortem noted that a silent `packages[""].devDependencies.zod` mirror-entry drift had been re-added during an unrelated install. Any `npm install` run in this story's dev agent shell could trip the same class of defect on `package.json`'s 24 devDependencies. AC 9's `sha256sum` belt-and-braces check catches it. Rationale: the five removed entries are `scripts` keys, not `dependencies` / `devDependencies` — npm's lockfile does not track scripts, so the lockfile SHOULD be byte-identical. If it isn't, something else changed.
201	
202	**(I) Prettier's `prettier-plugin-packagejson` sort order matters.** The plugin sorts the `scripts` object alphabetically by key (observable in `package.json:35-67`). Deleting five keys should not perturb sort order of the remaining 23; `prettier --check` via the `format:check` subcommand is the enforcement gate. If the dev agent's manual edit disturbs whitespace or sort order, `format:check` catches it before commit — do NOT `--no-verify` the hook; fix the edit.
203

Without that merge, the `release:*` stubs were still pointing at workflows that existed (even though deprecated), so deletion would have orphaned the "see release.yaml" pointer for operators. Story 6.1 closing is what makes this story's "warning is vacuous" framing correct.

That story intentionally kept them as fail-loud stubs; this story now retires the stubs per Story 4.2's own forward-reference ("a post-v1.0.0 cleanup pass removes them outright" is literally echoed in `CONTRIBUTING.md:85`'s text). Clean closure of the Story 4.2 → Story 6.2 arc.

That published tarball is untouched by this repo edit — the registry-vs-repo divergence is expected and correct (the registry is the historical ground truth for what v1.0.0 shipped; the repo's current `package.json` is the source of truth for what v1.0.1+ will ship).
212	- **Story 1.1 branch protection on `main`** — AC 10 PR-only constraint.
213	- **Epic 5 retrospective (2026-04-24) set Commitments 3, 5** — live at create-story. Commitment 3 → AC 6(c) issue-#211 non-bundling; Commitment 5 → the single-commit, single-PR, no-bundling framing of the whole story.

Story 6-3's pre-flight check SHOULD confirm that no workflow on `main` reads `secrets.NPM_TOKEN` (Story 6.1 removed the two workflows that did; `release.yaml` uses OIDC, not the token) AND that no `package.json` script reads it (Story 6.2 removes the last script layer that might have implied fallback-to-token behavior, though none of the stubs actually read the secret — the body was `exit 1` before it could reach any npm operation).

A consumer running `npm install bmad-module-skill-forge` will not see the stale stubs in their install tree's `scripts` section.

253	## Dev Notes
254	
255	### What this story actually is
256	
257	A **surgical one-file doc reconciliation + one pre-PR `gh` call** covering the final FR19 clause (remove `NPM_TOKEN` secret from repo settings). One commit, one PR, one file edited (`docs/RELEASING.md`), one off-repo state change (`gh secret delete NPM_TOKEN`) executed BEFORE the PR opens so the PR's doc prose describes the post-deletion state accurately. Closes Epic 6 (three-story cleanup arc: 6.1 workflows → 6.2 scripts → 6.3 secret) and embeds the epic's required close-out summary ("SKF's npm publish credential surface is now OIDC-only. Future incidents requiring credential revocation involve npmjs.com's trusted-publisher config, not a repo secret.") verbatim into `docs/RELEASING.md` as the permanent canonical home.
258	
259	The deletion is safe NOW because v1.0.0 shipped via OIDC successfully (Epic 5 retro Commitment 4 — the secret existed as the rollback path during the migration; v1.0.0 launch operationalized OIDC as the canonical path). The four `docs/RELEASING.md` prose edits reconcile stale "still exists / has not yet been removed / is removed post-v1.0.0 (see Story 6.3)" claims to past-tense current-state prose — if this story did NOT update those prose claims, the canonical release doc would contradict the merged repo state (the token is gone, but the doc says "remains"), which is the exact "spec drift" class Epic 4 retro #8 warned against.
260	
261	**What this story is NOT:**
262	
263	- NOT a release. Zero `release.yaml` dispatch, zero `install-smoke.yaml` dispatch, zero npm registry state change. NFR6 v1.0.0 immutability is vacuously respected (the registry's published v1.0.0 tarball is byte-frozen on npm and cannot be affected by repo-secret deletion or doc edits).
264	- NOT a workflow edit. `release.yaml`'s `NPM_TOKEN: ""` empty-string env overrides at `:191` and `:688` stay — they are FR4 defense-in-depth against runner-env token leakage, not `secrets.*` reads. Deleting the repo secret has zero effect on their operational role.
265	- NOT a npm Trusted Publisher edit. The OIDC config on `npmjs.com → package Settings → Trusted Publisher` is a separate credential surface; `gh secret delete` does not touch it. Its continued correctness is what makes the repo-secret deletion safe.
266	- NOT a `release` GitHub Environment edit. Story 1.2 ensured the env has zero env-scoped secrets; AC 2(e) + AC 7 confirm the invariance holds pre- and post-deletion.
267	- NOT a `CONTRIBUTING.md` / `README.md` / `CHANGELOG.md` edit. AC 2(h) recon confirmed no "Story 6.3 still pending" claims exist outside `docs/RELEASING.md`.
268	- NOT a `release-audits/v1.0.0-launch-audit.md` edit. The `:728` forward-looking Story 6.3 reference is historically valid (records the state at Epic 5 close — Stories 6.1/6.2/6.3 were unblocked at that point) and stays per audit-artifact preservation principle.
269	- NOT a retrospective. `epic-6-retrospective` stays `optional` unless Armel explicitly runs one.
270	- NOT a bundled PR. Per Commitment 5, none of Epic 5 retro P1 / P2 / P7 standing flags or issue #211 get bundled here.
271	
272	### Load-bearing decisions already made at create-story (no Dev-Agent re-derivation needed)
273	
274	**(A) `gh secret delete` runs BEFORE the PR, not after.** Sequencing rationale: the PR's doc prose describes the post-deletion state ("The token was removed from repo-level secrets in Story 6.3"). If the deletion happened after the PR merged, the doc would briefly describe a state that doesn't yet exist (while the PR is open on the branch). Pre-PR ordering keeps the merge's state claim accurate from the merge-commit moment onward. Secondary rationale: the pre-PR `gh secret list` transcript is the machine-checkable deletion evidence that goes into the PR body per AC 3(c) — without that pre-PR ordering, the PR body would be citing a future event as evidence, which is unauditable. Tertiary: the `gh secret delete` command is a single irreversible API call (the token itself is the only piece of unrecoverable state — recreating it requires generating a new npm token and pasting it; this story's scope does not include preserving the token value). Running it pre-PR lets the dev agent commit to the irreversible action with clear eyes before a reviewer sees the PR description.
275	
276	**(B) Four prose edits, not one comprehensive doc rewrite.** `docs/RELEASING.md` is ~880 lines; the four `NPM_TOKEN`-related prose references (`:80`, `:85`, `:157`, `:177`, `:426-427`) are surgical edit points within otherwise-correct prose. A comprehensive rewrite would trigger the Epic 5 retro Commitment 5 no-bundling alarm (scope creep beyond "remove token + reconcile doc") and risk introducing prose defects in sections that are currently correct. The four targeted edits maintain the doc's existing structure; any restructuring is future work. `:80` table row + `:157` OIDC-overview statement both stay UNCHANGED — the review-only confirmation that they don't need editing is AC 5(a) + AC 5(e).
277	
278	**(C) `:177` replacement embeds the epic close-out sentence verbatim.** Per epic AC "When I commit a final summary Then the Epic 6 close-out SHALL note: 'SKF's npm publish credential surface is now OIDC-only. Future incidents requiring credential revocation involve npmjs.com's trusted-publisher config, not a repo secret.'" — the natural permanent home is `docs/RELEASING.md § npm Trusted Publisher` since that section already discusses the OIDC-vs-token trade-off. Embedding the sentence there (not just in the PR body) makes it survive PR archival and gives future maintainers a single canonical doc to read for the post-Story-6.3 credential-surface invariant.
279	
280	**(D) `:426-427` consolidation, not preservation.** The current two bullets at `:426` and `:427` each assume a live `NPM_TOKEN` secret exists (the two-bullet structure was calibrated for the pre-Story-6.3 state where token-compromise and OIDC-compromise were two concurrent active incident classes). Post-deletion, the "token compromise" incident class is vestigial under normal operation — it only fires under an OIDC-incident exception-path that temporarily re-adds the token. The consolidated single bullet matches that reality: the token-compromise response is scoped to the exception-path scenario, not a default operational class. Deleting one bullet outright and preserving the other would leave the remaining bullet implying it's the only incident class that matters, which misreads the incident-class taxonomy.
281	
282	**(E) `release.yaml:191` + `:688` `NPM_TOKEN: ""` stay.** These lines are NOT `secrets.NPM_TOKEN` reads — they are literal empty-string env assignments. Their operational purpose is FR4 defense-in-depth: if `npm publish` auto-picks-up an `NPM_TOKEN` from the runner env (via `.npmrc` inheritance, a pre-step that set it, or a machine-user's config), the empty-string override ensures npm's OIDC-first auth path is not accidentally bypassed by a stale token. Deleting the repo secret does not remove this class of risk (other leakage paths could still introduce a runner-env `NPM_TOKEN`); the empty-string overrides remain load-bearing regardless. AC 5(c) prose update explicitly protects them from being ambiguously dragged into "the token is gone" framing.
283	
284	**(F) Sprint-status `epic-6: in-progress → done` transition is Story-6.3-scope, not retro-scope.** Per sprint-status legend, epic `in-progress → done` is manual when all stories complete. Story 6.3 is the third and final Epic 6 story; when it reaches `done`, the epic closes. The code-review workflow typically handles story-level `review → done`; whether it also covers epic-level flips depends on the skill's scope. If not, the dev agent documents the epic-flip as an explicit TODO in Completion Notes so it doesn't slip through and create a stale `epic-6: in-progress` entry that Epic 5 retro Commitment 15 would have to sweep later.
285	
286	**(G) Branch name `feat/v1-cleanup-npm-token` follows Story 5.3/5.4/6.1/6.2 convention.** Prefix `feat/v1-` keeps the v1-era cleanup branches grouped; `cleanup-npm-token` is specific enough to distinguish from Story 6.1's `feat/v1-cleanup-legacy-workflows` and Story 6.2's `feat/v1-cleanup-release-scripts`. Do NOT reuse any prior Epic 6 branch.
287	
288	**(H) The `release.yaml` two-commit-per-release topology (post-Story 3.4 PR-auto-merge) is unaffected.** `release.yaml` authenticates via OIDC (job-level `permissions: id-token: write` at `:27`) and the publish steps at `:199` (`npm publish --dry-run --tag "$TAG"`) and `:691-694` (final `npm publish --tag <channel>`) never consumed `secrets.NPM_TOKEN`. Pre-flight recon verified this by grep; post-deletion the workflow's next dispatch (whenever that is — maybe v1.0.1 or v1.1.0) SHALL publish via OIDC just as it did for v1.0.0 and rc.3. AC 11(f) npm-view sanity check provides supporting evidence.
289	
290	**(I) The epic's "Given all three cleanup stories (6.1, 6.2, 6.3) are complete" conjunction-style AC is satisfied by this story's merge.** Post-merge:
291	- Single canonical workflow at `.github/workflows/release.yaml` ✓ (Story 6.1)
292	- No `release:*` scripts in `package.json` ✓ (Story 6.2)
293	- No `NPM_TOKEN` secret in repo settings ✓ (Story 6.3, this story)
294	- No long-lived npm publish credential exists anywhere in SKF's infrastructure ✓ (conjunction of the above three, validated by the AC 11(a)-(e) audits)
295	
296	The PR body per AC 3(f) SHOULD enumerate these four invariants verbatim as the epic close-out checklist.
