> AGENT

I've read all three stories end-to-end. Given Epic 6 was three mechanical cleanup stories (not design-decision work like Epics 1-5), I'll skip the party-mode dialogue and run a lean synthesis retro — matches the tone of prior SKF retros. Here's what I pulled out; push back on anything that's wrong or missing.

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
🔄 EPIC 6 RETROSPECTIVE — Post-v1.0.0 Cleanup
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

**Epic status:** all three stories `done`; `epic-6: done` already flipped by Story 6.3's code-review pass (per sprint-status `last_updated` at `:38`); retro status `optional` → `done` on save.
**Previous retro:** Epic 5 (2026-04-24).
**Next epic:** **none** — `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/epics.md` defines exactly 6 epics and Epic 6 was the terminal Post-v1.0.0 Cleanup. FR19 closes; Epic 6's own AC ("no long-lived npm publish credential exists anywhere in SKF's infrastructure") is satisfied. Future work is post-epic housekeeping (P1–P7 standing flags, F1–G4 hardening candidates, issue #211, and the deferred Epic 6 review-findings docs-polish PR).

## Summary — one day, three clean merges

The whole epic shipped on **2026-04-24** in a tight ~6.5-hour window:

| Story | Merged | Commit | PR | Envelope | CI |
|-------|--------|--------|----|----|----|
| 6.1 legacy workflow deletion | 12:55:35Z | `309f558` | #222 | 4 files (-243/+3) | 7/7 PASS, first attempt |
| 6.2 `release:*` script removal | 15:50:58Z | `d531945` | #223 | 2 files (-6/+0) | 7/7 PASS, first attempt |
| 6.3 `NPM_TOKEN` removal + doc reconcile | 19:32:05Z | `45bed3a` | #224 | 1 file (-1 net) + off-repo secret delete | 7/7 PASS, first attempt |

**Headline:** zero pipeline defects, zero re-dispatches, zero envelope violations across three stories. Dramatic contrast to Epic 5 Story 5.2's 7-attempt saga. FR19 fully closed — SKF now has single-rooted release.yaml, zero `release:*` scripts on `main`, zero `NPM_TOKEN` at any scope, zero long-lived npm publish credentials anywhere.

## What went well

1. **Epic 5 retro commitments were load-bearing, not archival.** Every Epic 6 story imported specific commitments as AC dependencies:
   - Commitment 1 (precondition recon with verbatim transcript) → AC 2 in all three stories
   - Commitment 2 (shell-injection surface closure) → 6.1 PR body rationale
   - Commitment 3 (no issue #211 bundling) → explicit decline in all three NOT-bundled statements
   - Commitment 4 (OIDC-shipped enabling precondition) → 6.3 PR body with Story 5.3 run URL + SLSA attestation
   - Commitment 5 (no-bundling, separate PRs) → 3-for-3 envelope discipline held
   - Commitment 9 (adversarial review every story) → 3-for-3
   - Commitment 15 (sprint-status retro-housekeeping) → 6.3 completion note flagged `epic-6 → done` for code-review, which closed it cleanly
2. **Pre-execution scope envelopes held 3-for-3 exactly.** 6.1=4 files (2 deletions + 2 prunes), 6.2=2 files (package.json + CONTRIBUTING.md), 6.3=1 file (docs/RELEASING.md) + 1 off-repo secret delete. Zero surprises, zero scope creep.
3. **"Retire the deprecation warning when the deprecated thing is retired" pattern held.** Story 6.1 removed CONTRIBUTING.md's "do not invoke publish.yaml/manual-release.yaml" bullet because the files were gone; Story 6.2 removed the "do not use npm run release:*" bullet because the scripts were gone. Vacuous warnings → deleted outright, no replacement bullet (git log is the audit log).
4. **Story 6.3's "off-repo state change before PR opens" ordering was the right call.** `gh secret delete` ran pre-PR so the doc prose describes the post-deletion state accurately and the PR body carries the pre/post `gh secret list` transcript as machine-checkable deletion evidence. If deletion happened post-merge, the merged doc would briefly describe a future-tense state.
5. **Historical-preservation principle held 3-for-3.** 6.1 AC 7(d) preserved `docs/RELEASING.md:431` Story 3.3 Patch A reference. 6.2 AC 6(a) preserved `release-audits/v1.0.0-launch-audit.md:123` stub transcript. 6.3 AC 6(b) preserved `release-audits/...:728` forward-looking Story 6.3 reference. Audit artifacts remain point-in-time records — they do not follow repo state forward.
6. **Epic 6 close-out sentence has a permanent doc home.** "SKF's npm publish credential surface is now OIDC-only. Future incidents requiring credential revocation involve npmjs.com's trusted-publisher config, not a repo secret." — embedded verbatim at `docs/RELEASING.md:177` (permanent) and in PR #224 body (ephemeral). Future maintainers find the invariant stated clearly.
7. **"Deletion is the fix" insight on HIGH shell-injection surface.** `manual-release.yaml:59-61` carried unshielded `${{ github.event.inputs.version_bump }}` in bash `case` — a HIGH-severity supply-chain risk that Story 5.4's `env:` injection pattern would normally patch. Story 6.1 deleted the file outright, which is a stronger fix than patching (no regression surface). The shell-injection closure is a free side-benefit of FR19's primary scope, cited in PR #222's rationale.
8. **Adversarial review continued delivering signal, even on mechanical cleanup.** Story 6.1: 0 patches, 2 defers, 9 dismissed — auditor NO FINDINGS on all 15 ACs. Story 6.3: 0 patches, 2 defers, 9 dismissed, but surfaced **6 real deferred docs-polish items** clustered in `RELEASING.md § npm Trusted Publisher` and `§ Rollback Playbook` (brittle line-number pins, circular cross-refs, broken `:80` Ctrl-F path post-edit, unguarded `NPM_TOKEN: ""` defense-in-depth). Pattern holds 18-for-18 across Epics 1–6.

## What didn't go well / challenges

1. **Story 6.3 left 6 deferred docs-polish items with no scheduled home.** They cluster in `RELEASING.md § npm Trusted Publisher` + `§ Rollback Playbook`. Real defects, not noise: brittle `release.yaml:191+688` line-number citations at `:177` with no CI guard; `:426` incident-response bullet tells operators to run `gh secret delete NPM_TOKEN` against a state "has no NPM_TOKEN to compromise" (404 = safe? or broken? ambiguous); `:80` table row cross-reference "see NPM_TOKEN note below" no longer lands on a NPM_TOKEN-led paragraph. Disposition was "bundle into one follow-up PR" — but that PR doesn't exist. Carry-forward.
2. **`NPM_TOKEN: ""` defense-in-depth at `release.yaml:191/688` has zero enforcement.** Story 6.3 prose claims these lines "stay load-bearing regardless of the secret's presence or absence" but nothing stops a future cleanup PR from silently deleting them. Edge Case Hunter suggested inline `# DO NOT REMOVE — FR4 defense-in-depth` comments; deferred as out-of-scope for 6.3's single-file envelope.
3. **Brittle line-number citations in prose** — same class as Epic 5 retro Commitment 13(a) "topology claims go stale when the workflow changes." `docs/RELEASING.md:177` pins specific `:191` and `:688` lines in `release.yaml`; any workflow edit silently invalidates without a CI guard. The pattern recurred.
4. **Minor spec-vs-actual divergence: line-count estimate.** Story 6.3 spec said `docs/RELEASING.md` is "~880 lines"; actual was 633. Absorbed by AC 5(f)'s ±5 tolerance on delta. Cosmetic, but another instance of Epic 5 retro Commitment 13(d) "char-count arithmetic errors" extended to line-count arithmetic.
5. **Story 6.3's "STANDING TODO for code-review" epic-closure pattern is fragile.** Story 6.3 completion notes flagged `epic-6: in-progress → done` as "deferred to code-review workflow or manual sweep" because dev-story's terminal is `review`, not `done`. This worked — code-review did flip it — but the hand-off was convention, not enforced. If the code-review skill had different scope, the flag would have gone stale.

## Patterns that held across Epics 1–6

- **Pre-execution scope envelope naming:** 18-for-18 stories (including Epic 6's three).
- **Adversarial code review with Patch/Defer/Dismiss triage:** 18-for-18.
- **Live recon transcript at Task 1, verbatim in Dev Agent Record:** 18-for-18.
- **ACs with inline CLI dry-run at story-authoring time:** mostly held; Story 6.3's `wc -l` estimate was the latest divergence instance (Commitment 12 carry-forward).
- **Downstream story validation as done-signal** (Epic 5 Commitment 14): n/a in Epic 6 (no downstream workflow-code deliverables).

## New Epic 6 commitments

1. **Schedule the deferred `docs/RELEASING.md` polish PR.** 6 concrete items from Story 6.3 review findings: (a) replace `release.yaml:191+688` line-number citations at `:177` with a symbolic anchor or a CI guard; (b) resolve `:426` incident-response ambiguity (404-means-safe vs 404-means-wrong-scope branching); (c) remove circular cross-reference within `:426` bullet; (d) fix `:80` table row cross-ref now that `:85` no longer leads with NPM_TOKEN; (e) add `# DO NOT REMOVE — FR4 defense-in-depth` comments at `release.yaml:191/688`; (f) add auditable `gh secret list | grep -i npm_token` command near `:85` invariant. Bundle into one PR (not six). Natural cadence: within the next release window when `docs/RELEASING.md` is next touched OR as a dedicated `chore(docs): RELEASING.md post-Epic-6 polish` PR.

2. **Add cross-file line-number validator or adopt a symbolic-anchor convention.** The Epic 5 retro Commitment 13(a) "topology claims go stale" pattern recurred in Epic 6 at `docs/RELEASING.md:177`'s `release.yaml:191, 688` citations. Two paths: (i) a simple `tools/validate-doc-refs.js` that scans `docs/` for `<filename>:<N>` patterns and verifies the line still contains the expected content; (ii) symbolic anchors (e.g., `release.yaml § "NPM_TOKEN: "" defense-in-depth"`) that don't pin line numbers. Path (i) is cheaper but requires tool authorship; path (ii) is convention-only but requires author discipline. **Recommendation:** (ii) first (convention change, zero tool cost), escalate to (i) only if a third occurrence recurs.

3. **Idempotent pre-PR `gh` operations pattern is worth generalizing.** Story 6.3's AC 4 explicitly documented the `gh secret delete` idempotency behavior (non-zero exit + "could not find secret" on already-absent) and branched the Dev Agent Record narrative accordingly. Future off-repo state changes (secret rotation, env config flips, dist-tag changes) should follow the same pattern: explicit pre-state recon → action → post-state recon → idempotency branch-aware dev log. Carry into any future "pre-PR gh operation" story.

4. **"Retire the deprecation warning when the deprecated thing is retired" is a reusable spec pattern.** Story 6.1 Dev Notes (C) and Story 6.2 Dev Notes (C) both invoked this principle: CONTRIBUTING.md bullets warning against invoking soon-to-be-deleted artifacts get deleted outright (no replacement bullet — the nonexistent artifact's error is a louder warning than any prose). Apply to any future "remove deprecated X" story.

5. **Epic-level `in-progress → done` flip is code-review-scope convention, not enforcement.** Worked in Epic 6 but is convention, not contract. If Armel's code-review skill scope changes, the epic-flip could silently fail. **Recommendation:** either codify into the dev-story workflow's "last story in epic" branch OR add an explicit check in retrospective workflow's Step 11 that sweeps for stale `epic-N: in-progress` when all stories are `done`. The retro workflow already does this per Commitment 15 — **so treat Commitment 15 as the enforcement mechanism, not code-review**. Document it explicitly.

The nonexistent artifact's error (workflow-not-found / `Missing script:`) is a louder warning than any prose bullet, and git log is the audit log. Current-state doc prose (`docs/RELEASING.md:85, 177, 426-427`) describes the present tense and MUST be reconciled when repo state changes. Story 6.3 had to make this distinction seven times in AC 5 + AC 6. The clear rule: **if it reads \"this happened,\" preserve; if it reads \"this is,\" reconcile.**\n\n## Load-bearing inputs carried forward (post-Epic-6)\n\nEpic 6 was the terminal epic. There is no Epic 7. What remains is post-epic discretionary housekeeping across several buckets.\n\n### Commitment-1 docs-polish PR (scheduled for this session)\n\nFrom Story 6.3's review findings, six concrete items bundle into one follow-up PR:\n\n- **(a) `docs/RELEASING.md:177` brittle line-number citations.** Prose cites `release.yaml:191 + 688` — any workflow edit silently invalidates with no CI guard. Replace with a symbolic anchor (e.g., \"the two `NPM_TOKEN: \"\"` env overrides in `release.yaml`\") that doesn't pin line numbers.\n- **(b) `docs/RELEASING.md:426` incident-response 404-ambiguity.** An operator under incident pressure runs `gh secret delete NPM_TOKEN` on the vestigial-path bullet, gets `could not find secret` / 404, and cannot tell if that means \"safe, already gone\" or \"wrong scope, broken.\" Resolve with explicit branching guidance.\n- **(c) `docs/RELEASING.md:426` circular cross-reference.** Bullet says \"see exception-path framing in § npm Trusted Publisher\" AND \"(documented in § npm Trusted Publisher)\" in one bullet — duplicate self-reference. `§ npm Trusted Publisher` defers back without defining the exception-path procedure itself. Collapse to one reference.\n- **(d) `docs/RELEASING.md:80` table row cross-reference.** Post-Story-6.3, `:85` now leads with \"**No secret is scoped to this environment.**\" — the `:80` table row's \"see `NPM_TOKEN` note below\" Ctrl-F path no longer lands on a NPM_TOKEN-led paragraph.\n- **(e) `release.yaml:191` + `:688` unguarded `NPM_TOKEN: \"\"` defense-in-depth.** Prose claims these lines \"stay load-bearing regardless of the secret's presence or absence\" but nothing prevents a future cleanup PR from silently deleting them. Add inline `# DO NOT REMOVE — FR4 defense-in-depth; see docs/RELEASING.md § npm Trusted Publisher` comments at both sites.\n- **(f) `docs/RELEASING.md:85` unauditable invariant.** Invariant (\"zero env-scoped secrets AND zero `NPM_TOKEN`-shaped secrets at any scope\") stated but not auditable from within the doc. Add a small audit-command snippet (e.g., `gh secret list --repo armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge | grep -ci npm_token` expected `0`) adjacent to the invariant.\n\nTotal envelope estimate: two files (`docs/RELEASING.md` + `.github/workflows/release.yaml`), ~10–15 edited lines, one commit, one PR. Scheduled for same-session execution as Phase 2 of this retro.\n\n### Epic 5 retro pre-flight items — still all open, none blocking\n\nEpic 6 did not address any of the four Epic 5 pre-flight items (P1–P4) because all were explicitly declined-scope per Commitment 5 no-bundling discipline:\n\n- **P1 — `rc` dist-tag still pinned at `1.0.0-rc.3` post-GA.** Users running `npm install bmad-module-skill-forge@rc` remain on the RC. **Recommendation:** `npm dist-tag rm bmad-module-skill-forge rc`. Not blocking; low-stakes one-liner ops task. Natural fit for the next release-ops sitting.\n- **P2 — GH Actions default 90-day log retention rots audit evidence.** `release-audits/v1.0.0-launch-audit.md` references run URLs (`24852899833`, `24856988815`, `24858754152`) as primary evidence. Pruning date is roughly **2026-07-22**. Mitigation: copy raw per-leg stdout into the audit artifact verbatim, or upload `install-smoke.yaml` summary artifact with 400-day retention.\n- **P3 — NFR9 needs automation, not policy softening.** Add `on: workflow_run: { workflows: [Release], types: [completed] }` OR `on: release: { types: [published] }` trigger to `install-smoke.yaml` so it auto-dispatches post-release. One-time workflow edit; permanent NFR9 enforcement. Fires on next release cut (v1.0.1 / v1.1.0).\n- **P4 — Orphaned `feat/release-workflow` branch + `v0.10.1-alpha.0` tag.** Do NOT delete until GitHub retention behavior on orphaned commits is confirmed via a throwaway test. Standing flag; no fire date.\n\n### Standing flags (no fire date)\n\n- **P5 — `prevent_self_review: false → true` + revoke solo-maintainer ruleset bypass.** Fires when a second reviewer onboards. Commitment 16 in Epic 5 retro.\n- **P6 — `install.js`/`update.js` silent-exit-0 pattern watch.** Add ESLint rule or `tools/validate-*` check if a third instance recurs.\n- **P7 — `package.json.main` points at side-effectful CLI entrypoint.** Declined in Story 6.2 per Commitment 5 blast-radius discipline. Standing flag; addressable in a dedicated \"programmatic-API-surface reconciliation\" story if STABILITY.md ever grows a Path A.\n- **P8 — Cross-file anchor validator + `CONTRIBUTING.md` → `docs/` link coverage.** Bundle with P2 audit-evidence hardening if done together. See also Commitment 2 below which subsumes this as a more general topology-pin validator.\n- **Release-workflow hardening F1–F6 + supply-chain G1–G4** from Epic 3/4 retros — queued for a post-v1.0.0 dedicated hardening story; not blocking.\n- **Issue #211** (piped-stdin install infeasibility) — separate CLI-UX ticket. Non-blocking per Epic 5 retro recommendation (b): document in `docs/RELEASING.md` that piped stdin isn't supported and canonical smoke test is `install-smoke.yaml`.\n\n### Deferred items from Epic 5 still in deferred-work.md (~60+ items across Stories 3-5 / 5-1 / 5-2 / 5-3 / 5-4)\n\nAll hardening-story territory. No fire date. Bundle when touching adjacent files.\n\n## Commitments\n\n### Carry-forward (immediate)\n\n1. **Execute the Commitment-1 docs-polish PR in the same session as this retro save.** Six items enumerated above. Single PR, single commit, two files (`docs/RELEASING.md` + `.github/workflows/release.yaml`). Rationale: context is hot (Story 6.3 review findings are in head-cache); deferring means paying ~10–15 minutes of context-reload cost later. Retro ships without an open IOU.\n\n2. **Adopt symbolic-anchor convention for cross-file prose references.** Epic 5 retro Commitment 13(a) \"topology claims go stale when the workflow changes\" recurred in Epic 6 at `docs/RELEASING.md:177`'s `release.yaml:191, 688` pin. Going forward: when prose must reference a specific code site in another file, use a **symbolic anchor** (\"the two `NPM_TOKEN: \"\"` env overrides in `release.yaml`\") rather than a line-number pin (`release.yaml:191 + 688`). Line numbers go stale silently; symbolic anchors go stale loudly (the referenced symbol disappears, which grep catches). Zero tool cost; convention only. Escalate to a `tools/validate-doc-refs.js` build only if a **third** occurrence recurs — the bar is \"three strikes, then tool.\"\n\n### Process improvements (continue across post-Epic-6 work)\n\n3. **Idempotent pre-PR `gh` operation pattern is reusable.** Story 6.3 AC 4 explicitly documented `gh secret delete`'s idempotency (non-zero exit + \"could not find secret\" on already-absent) and branched the Dev Agent Record narrative accordingly. Future off-repo state changes (secret rotation, env config flips, dist-tag changes, webhook adds/removes) should follow the same four-step pattern: **(a) pre-state recon transcript → (b) action → (c) post-state recon transcript → (d) idempotency-branch-aware dev log.** Cheap discipline; high audit-trail value.\n\n4. **\"Retire the deprecation warning when the deprecated thing is retired\" is a reusable spec pattern.** Apply to any future \"remove deprecated X\" story where `CONTRIBUTING.md` / `docs/*.md` carry a prose warning whose premise is the deprecated artifact's continued existence. Delete the warning outright; do not add a replacement bullet. The artifact's own absence (`workflow not found` / `Missing script:` / 404) is a louder warning than prose.\n\n5. **Epic-level `in-progress → done` flip enforcement lives in the retrospective workflow.** Per Epic 5 retro Commitment 15, sprint-status stale-entry sweeps are retro territory. Story 6.3's completion notes deferred `epic-6: in-progress → done` to \"code-review workflow or retro or manual sweep\" — this worked, but was convention, not enforced. **Canonical answer:** treat Commitment 15 as the enforcement mechanism. Retrospective workflow's Step 11 already sweeps for stale entries. Document this explicitly — code-review may or may not cover epic-level flips depending on skill scope; retrospective always does.\n\n### Adversarial review discipline (unchanged from Epic 5 Commitment 9)\n\n6. **Adversarial code review on every story, with explicit Patch / Defer / Dismiss triage — including mechanical cleanup.** Tempting to skip on deletions; Story 6.3 caught 6 real docs-polish defects that would have rotted silently. **19-for-19 will be 19-for-19** when the Commitment-1 polish PR ships; keep the discipline even on one-line changes.\n\n### Standing flags (no fire date) — carried forward from Epic 5 retro unchanged\n\n7. **Commitment 16 — `prevent_self_review: false → true` + revoke solo bypass.** Fires on second-reviewer onboarding.\n8. **Commitment 17 — `install.js` / `update.js` silent-exit-0 pattern watch.** Add ESLint rule or `tools/validate-*` check on third occurrence.\n9. **Commitment 18 — `package.json.main` side-effectful CLI entrypoint.** Declined in Story 6.2 per no-bundling. Addressable in a dedicated programmatic-API story.\n10. **Commitment 19 — Orphaned commit `2a57dcbd` + branch `feat/release-workflow`.** Do NOT delete until GH retention behavior on orphaned commits is confirmed via throwaway test.\n11. **Commitment 20 — Cross-file anchor validator covering `CONTRIBUTING.md` → `docs/` links.** Subsumed by Commitment 2's symbolic-anchor convention for now; escalate to tooling on third strike.\n\n## Readiness assessment\n\n| Dimension | Status |\n|-----------|--------|\n| Story completion | ✅ 3/3 done (6.1 / 6.2 / 6.3) |\n| Epic status | ✅ `epic-6: done` in sprint-status |\n| Live artifacts | ✅ commits `309f558` (6.1) / `d531945` (6.2) / `45bed3a` (6.3) on `main` |\n| Adversarial code review | ✅ 3/3 — Story 6.1 formal 3-layer (0 patches, 2 defers, 9 dismissed, auditor NO FINDINGS); Story 6.2 passed cleanly; Story 6.3 formal 3-layer (0 patches, 6 defers bundled into Commitment 1, 9 dismissed) |\n| Scope envelope adherence | ✅ 3/3 exact — 4-file / 2-file / 1-file+secret |\n| Epic 5 retro carry-forward | ✅ 11/11 applicable commitments held; 9 standing flags still standing; 2 minor recurrences of Commitments 12 + 13 (benign) |\n| FR19 closure | ✅ all three clauses: legacy workflows deleted / `release:*` removed / `NPM_TOKEN` deleted |\n| npm credential surface | ✅ OIDC-only; zero long-lived tokens anywhere |\n| HIGH shell-injection in `manual-release.yaml` | ✅ eliminated as side effect of Story 6.1 deletion |\n| `release` GitHub Environment invariants | ✅ 0 env-scoped secrets preserved through all three stories |\n| NFR6 v1.0.0 immutability | ✅ vacuously respected — Epic 6 touched zero npm registry state |\n| Unresolved blockers for post-epic work | none — no next epic to block |\n| Deferred work with scheduled home | ⚠️ 6 docs-polish items from Story 6.3 review findings — Commitment 1 addresses in same session |\n| Sprint-status stale entries | ✅ none detected; `epic-6-retrospective: optional → done` on save |\n\nEpic 6 is truly closed. FR19 is delivered. SKF has a **single-rooted OIDC-only release surface with zero long-lived credentials at any scope** — the security-posture headline that justified the three-story cleanup arc. The only loose thread is the Commitment-1 docs-polish PR, which ships same session.\n\n## Next steps\n\n1. **Save this retrospective**; flip `epic-6-retrospective: optional → done` in `sprint-status.yaml`. _(handled at retro save time)_\n\n2. **Execute Commitment-1 docs-polish PR** — six items bundled into `docs/RELEASING.md` + `.github/workflows/release.yaml` per above. Branch `feat/v1-releasing-md-post-epic-6-polish` (or similar) from `origin/main` at `45bed3a`. Single commit, single PR. _(scheduled for same session)_\n\n3. **No next epic exists.** Post-epic work is all discretionary:\n   - **Near-term release-ops candidates:** P1 (`rc` dist-tag removal / post-GA policy), P2 (audit-evidence copy-in before 2026-07-22 GH retention prunes run URLs), P3 (`install-smoke.yaml` auto-dispatch on release for NFR9 enforcement).\n   - **Standing flags** (P5–P8, Commitments 16–20): fire on trigger conditions (second reviewer onboarded, third recurrence of a pattern, etc.).\n   - **Issue #211** (piped-stdin install infeasibility): document in `docs/RELEASING.md` per Epic 5 retro recommendation (b), or leave open as won't-fix-with-rationale.\n   - **Hardening candidates F1–G4** from Epics 3–4 retros: queued for a dedicated post-v1.0.0 hardening story whenever the release pipeline is next actively touched.\n\n4. **Consider closing the project-level v1.0.0-launch arc formally.** Epic 5 delivered v1.0.0; Epic 6 cleaned up the deprecated surface around it. There is no Epic 7 in `epics.md`. If SKF's roadmap extends beyond \"v1.0.0 ship and maintain\" (e.g., v1.1.0 feature work, v2 planning), that's a PRD / epic-authoring step outside retrospective scope. If the roadmap genuinely is \"coast post-v1.0.0 with discretionary housekeeping,\" this retro and the Commitment-1 PR are the final formal artifacts of the v1.0.0 launch lineage.\n\n---\n\n**Team acknowledgement:** Epic 6 shipped clean because Epic 5's RC cycle absorbed all the pipeline defects on disposable numbers. The three-story cleanup arc is the reward for getting the upstream design right — single-rooted OIDC-only release surface, with zero long-lived credentials anywhere in SKF's infrastructure. v1.0.0 is live, provenanced, cross-platform verified, and now cleanly housekept.\n"
}

A job declaring `environment: release` pauses until a listed reviewer clicks "Approve and deploy" in the Actions UI.

77	| `prevent_self_review`      | `false` — see rationale below                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |

82	
83	**`prevent_self_review: false` — correctness constraint, not a loosened control.** Solo-maintainer setups cannot self-approve when this is `true`, so the gate would deadlock on any maintainer-triggered publish. The value flips to `true` the moment a second reviewer joins — do not leave it loose by inertia.
84	
85	**No secret is scoped to this environment.** The repo no longer carries an `NPM_TOKEN` secret at any scope (removed in Story 6.3 post-v1.0.0 once OIDC trusted publishing was operationally proven by the v1.0.0 launch). The invariant: the `release` env must have zero environment-scoped secrets, and the repo must have zero `NPM_TOKEN`-shaped secrets at any scope. If a future change scopes any secret to this environment, or if an `NPM_TOKEN` secret is ever re-added at repo scope, re-audit whether the OIDC trusted-publisher path is still in force — the OIDC path SHOULD be self-sufficient and a re-added token is a signal that something has regressed off the canonical path.

87	**Coupling with npm trusted publishing (Story 1.3).** The npm trusted publisher binds on four fields — `organization=armelhbobdad`, `repository=bmad-module-skill-forge`, `workflow filename=release.yaml`, `environment=release`. The environment name above is load-bearing: any rename here must be accompanied by a matching npm-side update in the same change, or the next publish returns 404.

170	
171	**Case-sensitive.** All four fields above use the exact lowercase forms shown; npm's matcher is an exact-string comparison. Do not capitalize on re-registration even if GitHub's UI surfaces a display-form with capitals.
172	
173	**Rename coupling — the four fields are load-bearing.** Renaming OR deleting the `release` GitHub environment (see § Release Environment), renaming or moving `release.yaml` within `.github/workflows/`, or flipping the extension between `.yaml` and `.yml` each require matching updates in the same PR to: (a) the npm-side Trusted Publisher, (b) the Registered table above, and (c) the `## Release Process` enumeration in `README.md` (which names both `release` and the Trusted Publisher). Skipping any of these produces an opaque `404` on the next publish — the error names the failure class, not the specific field.
174	
175	**Pre-registration inversion.** This entry was registered **before** `release.yaml` was authored (Story 3.1). The first live validator of the full OIDC chain is Story 3.2's alpha cut. If that cut's publish step 404s, open a **three-way comparison**: (1) the npm Settings tab, (2) the workflow YAML's `name` / `on` / `jobs.<id>.environment` lines, and (3) the Registered table above. The table is the ground truth because it captured the values at npm-save time — compare both the npm record and the workflow header against the table, never the workflow against itself (verifying the workflow against its own header will silently confirm a typo).
176	
177	**`NPM_TOKEN` is gone — OIDC is the only publish credential surface.** The token was removed from repo-level secrets in Story 6.3 (post-v1.0.0, once OIDC trusted publishing was operationally proven by the v1.0.0 launch). SKF's npm publish credential surface is now OIDC-only. Future incidents requiring credential revocation involve npmjs.com's trusted-publisher config, not a repo secret. `release.yaml` authenticates via Trusted Publisher OIDC and continues to set `NPM_TOKEN: ""` (lines 191 + 688) as defense-in-depth against a stale token being auto-picked-up by npm from the runner env — those literal empty-string assignments are not `secrets.*` reads and stay load-bearing regardless of the secret's presence or absence. If a future OIDC incident forces a last-resort token-based re-publish path, re-adding `NPM_TOKEN` at repo scope is the exception-path, not the default — document the flip in the commit body, file an issue for the OIDC incident class that required it, and remove the token again the moment OIDC is restored.
178	
179	**Fixing a bad registration.** The npm UI exposes both **Edit** and **Delete** on an existing Trusted Publisher entry (observed 2026-04-20). Prefer edit for a single-field typo; prefer delete-and-re-add if multiple fields are wrong or the edit form ever feels ambiguous. **Pre-Story 3.2**: there is no destructive side effect because no publish is attempted yet, and delete-and-re-add keeps the audit trail cleaner. **Post-Story 3.2**: a publish that fires during the delete-and-re-add window will 404 — gate any delete-and-re-add behind a manual publish freeze (pause any active `release.yaml` runs, confirm no tags are in-flight) before touching the entry.

A compromise discovered pre-v1.0.0 is recoverable via delete-and-re-register with no downstream-consumer blast radius (no stable release users yet). Post-v1.0.0, the incident has downstream blast radius and the `support@npmjs.com` coordination path is load-bearing.
425	- **Do-NOT clause — `NPM_TOKEN` rotation is not OIDC incident response.** Do NOT rotate `NPM_TOKEN` as a first response. The token is not on the OIDC path; rotating it does nothing to stop an OIDC compromise.
426	  - If an `NPM_TOKEN` exists at repo scope at the time of an incident (see exception-path framing in § npm Trusted Publisher), revoke it at `https://www.npmjs.com` → **Access Tokens** AND remove it from the repo: `gh secret delete NPM_TOKEN --repo armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge`. A compromised `NPM_TOKEN` would be a **separate incident class** from OIDC compromise. Story 6.3 removed the token in 2026-04 post-v1.0.0 so this path is vestigial under normal operation — the repo has no `NPM_TOKEN` to compromise. The bullet exists for the exception-path where an OIDC incident has forced a temporary token re-add (documented in § npm Trusted Publisher); revoke + delete still applies in that scenario. During a window where OIDC is revoked AND an exception-path token is also compromised, there is no valid publish path — the repo enters lockdown until Trusted Publisher is re-registered. Document the flip in the incident post-mortem; do not publish via any stale path.
427	
428	### Scenario G — "release.yaml disabled, reverted, or missing from main"
429	
430	- **Trigger.** `release.yaml` is the single-rooted release workflow (Story 3.3 Patch A neutralized the legacy `publish.yaml` `v*` tag trigger). If `release.yaml` is reverted, renamed, moved, or disabled at the repo-settings level (`gh workflow disable release.yaml`) and someone tries to cut a release, **no workflow fires**. Silent no-op until a maintainer checks `npm view` or the Actions UI.
431	- **Detection.** After any PR that touches `.github/workflows/*` — and before any release attempt — run:
432	
433	  ```bash
434	  gh api repos/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/actions/workflows \
435	    --jq '.workflows[] | select(.name=="Release") | {name, state, path}'
436	  # expected: {"name":"Release","state":"active","path":".github/workflows/release.yaml"}
437	  ```
438	
439	  If `state` is not `active` (the GitHub Actions API reports `disabled_manually` when disabled via the UI or `gh workflow disable`), or no matching workflow is returned at all (file renamed, moved, or deleted), Scenario G applies.
```

After `npm unpublish bmad-module-skill-forge@0.10.1`, the next `release.yaml` dispatch with `version_bump: patch` on `0.10.0` will produce `0.10.2`, **not** `0.10.1` again. Attempting `npm version 0.10.1 --no-git-tag-version` would drive the workflow's `npm publish` toward an npm-burned version and return `403`. Treat the unpublish as a one-way jump in the version number line, not a true undo.

> AGENT

Confirmed — this is a real miss. Here's the actual state:

**Stale:** `docs/_data/pinned.yaml:26` → `skf_version: "0.10.0"` while `package.json` is `1.0.0` and v1.0.0 is live on npm. The file's own header comment explicitly states "SKF package version — matches the `version` field in package.json" — so this is a documented invariant that's currently violated.

**Why nothing caught it — three-layer defense failure:**

1. **Story 5.1 pre-v1.0.0 readiness audit** should have caught it in the 8-file envelope sibling-sweep but didn't — the audit enumerated `package.json` + `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` as the version-bearing files and missed `pinned.yaml` entirely.
2. **`release.yaml` sync block** bumps `package.json` + `marketplace.json` on every release but doesn't touch `pinned.yaml:26` — the file was out-of-scope when Story 3.1 authored the workflow.
3. **`tools/validate-docs-drift.js`** cross-checks the four `oms-*` anchors (`skills:` list) and the `illustrative_libraries` list against oh-my-skills — but **never reads `skf_version`**. Earlier this session's `docs:validate-drift` green check (`OK: 4 skills checked`) was telling me the truth about those 4 skills and nothing about `skf_version`.

This is Epic 5 retro Commitment 13(a) "topology claims go stale" + Epic 6 retro Commitment 20 "cross-file anchor validator" — compound miss, both commitments proven load-bearing in one finding.

**Other `0.10.0` references in `docs/`** are all legitimate illustrative examples (getting-started.md line 74's `v0.10.0 → v1.0.0 available` is literally the upgrade-prompt example; RELEASING.md's `:202, :279, :588` use `0.10.0` as "the prior stable" in historical/example narrative) — none need editing.

**The fix has two layers:**

1. **Immediate** — one-line bump `pinned.yaml:26 "0.10.0" → "1.0.0"`. Tiny PR. Closes the current drift.
2. **Systemic close** — pick one or both:
   - **(a) Extend `validate-docs-drift.js`** to assert `pinned.yaml.skf_version === <package.json .version>`. Cheap (~10 lines); catches future drift at CI time but doesn't prevent write-side miss.
   - **(b) Add `pinned.yaml` to `release.yaml`'s bump-sync set** so every release atomically updates it alongside `package.json` + `marketplace.json`. This is the proper fix — same class of guarantee as the existing two sync targets.

Both together is the strongest posture: (b) prevents the miss on write, (a) catches it at CI if anything else ever sets the field.

**My recommendation:** do both in a single follow-up PR — they're the same feature (keep `pinned.yaml.skf_version` locked to `package.json.version`) from two angles. Want me to draft it now, or file as a standing flag for whenever you next touch the release pipeline?

357:          HEAD_SHA=$(gh pr view "$PR_NUMBER" --json headRefOid --jq .headRefOid)
362:            --paginate --jq '[.workflow_runs[] | select(.conclusion == "action_required") | .id]')
363:          COUNT=$(echo "$STUCK" | jq 'length')
369:          for RUN_ID in $(echo "$STUCK" | jq -r '.[]'); do
393:          HEAD_SHA=$(gh pr view "$PR_NUMBER" --json headRefOid --jq .headRefOid)
399:            --jq '[.rules[] | select(.type=="required_status_checks") | .parameters.required_status_checks[].context]' 2>/dev/null || echo "")
404:          REQUIRED_COUNT=$(echo "$REQUIRED_JSON" | jq 'length')
413:            REGISTERED=$(gh api "/repos/${{ github.repository }}/commits/$HEAD_SHA/check-runs" --paginate --jq '.check_runs | length' 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
432:              --paginate --jq '[.check_runs[] | {name, status, conclusion, started_at, html_url}]')
437:            # names. Unquoted `$(jq -r '.[]')` in a `for` loop word-splits on
442:              CTX_RUN=$(echo "$CHECKS" | jq -c --arg n "$CTX" '[.[] | select(.name==$n)] | sort_by(.started_at) | last')
448:              STATUS=$(echo "$CTX_RUN" | jq -r .status)
449:              CONCL=$(echo "$CTX_RUN" | jq -r .conclusion)
460:                  RUN_URL=$(echo "$CTX_RUN" | jq -r .html_url)
468:            done < <(echo "$REQUIRED_JSON" | jq -r '.[]')
530:            DECISION=$(echo "$VIEW" | jq -r .reviewDecision)
531:            STATE=$(echo "$VIEW" | jq -r .state)
535:              MERGE_SHA=$(echo "$VIEW" | jq -r '.mergeCommit.oid // "unknown"')
536:              MERGED_AT=$(echo "$VIEW" | jq -r '.mergedAt // "unknown"')

```
1	/**
2	 * Documentation Drift Validator
3	 *
4	 * Verifies that SKF docs agree with the canonical oh-my-skills output on
5	 * every version number, commit SHA, and library reference the docs cite.
6	 *
7	 * What it checks:
8	 * - Every skill listed in `docs/_data/pinned.yaml` actually exists at the
9	 *   claimed version and commit in $OMS/skills/<name>/<version>/<name>/metadata.json
10	 * - The metadata.json `version` field matches the anchor `version`
11	 * - The metadata.json `source_commit` field matches the anchor `source_commit`
12	 * - Every `.md` file under `docs/` is grepped for `<library> v?<x.y.z>` strings;
13	 *   any that don't match the currently-pinned version for that library are flagged
14	 * - Known illustrative libraries (hono, drizzle-orm, ...) are whitelisted and
15	 *   allowed to carry any version number
16	 *
17	 * Usage:
18	 *   node tools/validate-docs-drift.js             # validate, exit 1 on drift
19	 *   OMS=/path/to/oh-my-skills node tools/validate-docs-drift.js
20	 *
21	 * Exit codes:
22	 *   0 — no drift detected
23	 *   1 — drift found; fix the docs or update docs/_data/pinned.yaml
24	 *   2 — infrastructure failure (anchors file missing, oh-my-skills path
25	 *       unreachable, yaml parse error)
26	 */
27	
28	const fs = require('node:fs');
29	const path = require('node:path');
30	const yaml = require('js-yaml');
31	
32	const SKF_ROOT = path.resolve(__dirname, '..');
33	const DOCS_DIR = path.join(SKF_ROOT, 'docs');
34	const ANCHORS_PATH = path.join(DOCS_DIR, '_data', 'pinned.yaml');
35	
36	function loadAnchors() {
37	  if (!fs.existsSync(ANCHORS_PATH)) {
38	    console.error(`error: anchors file not found at ${ANCHORS_PATH}`);
39	    process.exit(2);
40	  }
41	  try {
42	    return yaml.load(fs.readFileSync(ANCHORS_PATH, 'utf8'));
43	  } catch (error) {
44	    console.error(`error: could not parse ${ANCHORS_PATH}: ${error.message}`);
45	    process.exit(2);
46	  }
47	}
48	
49	function resolveOmsPath(anchors) {
50	  if (process.env.OMS) {
51	    return path.resolve(process.env.OMS);
52	  }
53	  return path.resolve(SKF_ROOT, anchors.oh_my_skills_path);
54	}
55	
56	// Accept either a full SHA match or a short-SHA prefix match, per
57	// src/skf-update-skill/steps-c/step-03-re-extract.md:36 — the pinned commit
58	// is often stored as an 8-char short hash. Both anchors and metadata are
59	// lowercased before comparison; blank / null values never match.
60	function commitsMatch(anchorCommit, metadataCommit) {
61	  if (!anchorCommit || !metadataCommit) return false;
62	  const a = String(anchorCommit).toLowerCase();
63	  const b = String(metadataCommit).toLowerCase();
64	  return a === b || a.startsWith(b) || b.startsWith(a);
65	}
66	
67	function checkCanonicalFiles(anchors, omsPath) {
68	  const errors = [];
69	  if (!fs.existsSync(omsPath) || !fs.statSync(omsPath).isDirectory()) {
70	    errors.push(
71	      `CRITICAL: oh_my_skills_path does not resolve to a directory: ${omsPath}`,
72	      `  hint: set OMS=/path/to/oh-my-skills or edit docs/_data/pinned.yaml`,
73	    );
74	    return errors;
75	  }
76	
77	  for (const [skillName, spec] of Object.entries(anchors.skills)) {
78	    const metadataPath = path.join(omsPath, 'skills', skillName, spec.version, skillName, 'metadata.json');
79	
80	    if (!fs.existsSync(metadataPath)) {
81	      errors.push(`MISSING: ${skillName}@${spec.version} — expected ${metadataPath}`);
82	      continue;
83	    }
84	
85	    let metadata;
86	    try {
87	      metadata = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(metadataPath, 'utf8'));
88	    } catch (error) {
89	      errors.push(`UNPARSEABLE: ${metadataPath} — ${error.message}`);
90	      continue;
91	    }
92	
93	    if (metadata.version !== spec.version) {
94	      errors.push(`VERSION_DRIFT: ${skillName} — anchors say ${spec.version}, ` + `metadata.json says ${metadata.version}`);
95	    }
96	
97	    if (!commitsMatch(spec.source_commit, metadata.source_commit)) {
98	      const anchorShort = (spec.source_commit || '').slice(0, 12);
99	      const realShort = (metadata.source_commit || '').slice(0, 12);
100	      errors.push(`COMMIT_DRIFT: ${skillName} — anchors say ${anchorShort}, ` + `metadata.json says ${realShort}`);
101	    }
102	
103	    if (metadata.confidence_tier !== spec.confidence_tier) {
104	      errors.push(`TIER_DRIFT: ${skillName} — anchors say ${spec.confidence_tier}, ` + `metadata.json says ${metadata.confidence_tier}`);
105	    }
106	
107	    if (metadata.source_authority !== spec.source_authority) {
108	      errors.push(
109	        `AUTHORITY_DRIFT: ${skillName} — anchors say ${spec.source_authority}, ` + `metadata.json says ${metadata.source_authority}`,
110	      );
111	    }
112	  }
113	
114	  return errors;
115	}
116	
117	function getMarkdownFiles(dir) {
118	  const files = [];
119	  function walk(currentDir) {
120	    const entries = fs.readdirSync(currentDir, { withFileTypes: true });
121	    for (const entry of entries) {
122	      if (entry.name.startsWith('_')) continue;
123	      const fullPath = path.join(currentDir, entry.name);
124	      if (entry.isDirectory()) {
125	        walk(fullPath);
126	      } else if (entry.isFile() && entry.name.endsWith('.md')) {
127	        files.push(fullPath);
128	      }
129	    }
130	  }
131	  walk(dir);
132	  return files;
133	}
134	
135	function checkDocsForStaleVersions(anchors) {
136	  const errors = [];
137	
138	  // Build map: library-name → current-version
139	  const libVersions = Object.fromEntries(Object.values(anchors.skills).map((spec) => [spec.library, spec.version]));
140	  const libs = Object.keys(libVersions);
141	  if (libs.length === 0) return errors;
142	
143	  const illustrative = new Set(anchors.illustrative_libraries || []);
144	
145	  // Pattern: <library-name><whitespace or @>v?<semver>
146	  // Word-boundary anchored to avoid partial matches inside other words.
147	  const libPattern = String.raw`\b(` + libs.map((l) => l.replaceAll(/[.*+?^${}()|[\\]\\\\]/g, String.raw`\\$&`)).join('|') + ')';
148	  const versionPattern = String.raw`[\s@]+v?(\d+\.\d+\.\d+)`;
149	  const regex = new RegExp(libPattern + versionPattern, 'gi');
150	
151	  for (const mdFile of getMarkdownFiles(DOCS_DIR)) {
152	    const content = fs.readFileSync(mdFile, 'utf8');
153	    const lines = content.split('\n');
154	    for (const [idx, line] of lines.entries()) {
155	      for (const match of line.matchAll(regex)) {
156	        const lib = match[1].toLowerCase();
157	        if (illustrative.has(lib)) continue;
158	        const found = match[2];
159	        const expected = libVersions[lib];
160	        if (expected && found !== expected) {
161	          const relPath = path.relative(SKF_ROOT, mdFile);
162	          errors.push(`STALE_VERSION: ${relPath}:${idx + 1} — ${lib} v${found} (expected v${expected})`);
163	        }
164	      }
165	    }
166	  }
167	
168	  return errors;
169	}
170	
171	function main() {
172	  const anchors = loadAnchors();
173	  const omsPath = resolveOmsPath(anchors);
174	
175	  const errors = [...checkCanonicalFiles(anchors, omsPath), ...checkDocsForStaleVersions(anchors)];
176	
177	  if (errors.length > 0) {
178	    console.error('DRIFT DETECTED:\n');
179	    for (const err of errors) {
180	      console.error(`  - ${err}`);
181	    }
182	    console.error(`\n${errors.length} drift finding(s). Fix the docs or update docs/_data/pinned.yaml.`);
183	    console.error('\nAnchors file: docs/_data/pinned.yaml');
184	    console.error(`Checked against: ${omsPath}`);
185	    process.exit(1);
186	  }
187	
188	  const skillCount = Object.keys(anchors.skills).length;
189	  console.log(`OK: ${skillCount} skills checked against ${omsPath}, no drift.`);
190	  process.exit(0);
191	}
192	
193	main();
194	
```

105	
106	      - name: Configure Git
107	        run: |
108	          git config user.name "github-actions[bot]"
109	          git config user.email "github-actions[bot]@users.noreply.github.com"
110	
111	      - name: Bump version
112	        run: |
113	          case "${{ github.event.inputs.version_bump }}" in
114	            alpha|beta|rc) npm version prerelease --no-git-tag-version --preid=${{ github.event.inputs.version_bump }} ;;
115	            *)             npm version ${{ github.event.inputs.version_bump }} --no-git-tag-version ;;
116	          esac
117	
118	      - name: Get new version and previous tag
119	        id: version
120	        run: |
121	          echo "new_version=$(node -p "require('./package.json').version")" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
122	          echo "previous_tag=$(git describe --tags --abbrev=0)" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
123	
124	      - name: Update marketplace.json version
125	        run: |
126	          # Targeted JSON edit scoped to .plugins[0].version — resistant to future
127	          # schema additions of other `version` keys. Uses a temp file + atomic
128	          # rename so a partial-write mid-step cannot leave the file in a broken
129	          # shape (jq has no in-place edit). The VERSION variable is interpolated
130	          # via jq --arg to avoid shell-quote hazards.
131	          VERSION='${{ steps.version.outputs.new_version }}'
132	          jq --arg v "$VERSION" '.plugins[0].version = $v' .claude-plugin/marketplace.json > .claude-plugin/marketplace.json.tmp
133	          mv .claude-plugin/marketplace.json.tmp .claude-plugin/marketplace.json
134	          # Sanity check: the written version matches what we set.
135	          WROTE=$(jq -r '.plugins[0].version' .claude-plugin/marketplace.json)
136	          if [ "$WROTE" != "$VERSION" ]; then
137	            echo "::error::marketplace.json version write failed: expected $VERSION, got $WROTE"; exit 1
138	          fi
139	
140	      - name: Update CHANGELOG.md
141	        run: |
142	          npx conventional-changelog-cli -p conventionalcommits -i CHANGELOG.md -s
143	
144	      - name: Restore CHANGELOG preamble
145	        run: |
146	          # conventional-changelog-cli -s prepends the new release entry at byte 0
147	          # of CHANGELOG.md, pushing "# Changelog" title + preamble + "## [Unreleased]"
148	          # anchor DOWN below it. Restore the canonical Keep-a-Changelog shape:
149	          #   # Changelog
150	          #   (preamble)
151	          #   ## [Unreleased]
152	          #   ## [<new release>] (...)
153	          #   ## [<older releases>] (...)
154	          # Idempotent: if "# Changelog" is already on line 1 (exact match), no-op.
155	          [ -s CHANGELOG.md ] || { echo "::error::CHANGELOG.md is empty or missing — aborting release."; exit 1; }
156	          if ! head -n 1 CHANGELOG.md | grep -q '^# Changelog$'; then
157	            awk '
158	              BEGIN { new_entry=""; preamble=""; rest=""; section=0; seen_title=0 }
159	              # section 0: new release entry prepended by conventional-changelog -s
160	              # section 1: "# Changelog" title + preamble + "## [Unreleased]" anchor
161	              # section 2: first released version ("## [X.Y.Z]") onward
162	              section==0 && !seen_title && /^# Changelog$/ { seen_title=1; section=1 }
163	              section==1 && /^## \[[0-9]/ { section=2 }
164	              { if (section==0) new_entry=new_entry $0 "\n";
165	                else if (section==1) preamble=preamble $0 "\n";
166	                else rest=rest $0 "\n" }
167	              END {
168	                if (!seen_title) {
169	                  print "restore-preamble: # Changelog title not found in CHANGELOG.md — aborting" > "/dev/stderr"
170	                  exit 1
171	                }
172	                printf "%s%s%s", preamble, new_entry, rest
173	              }
174	            ' CHANGELOG.md > CHANGELOG.md.tmp
175	            [ -s CHANGELOG.md.tmp ] || { echo "::error::awk produced empty CHANGELOG.md.tmp — aborting release."; rm -f CHANGELOG.md.tmp; exit 1; }
176	            mv CHANGELOG.md.tmp CHANGELOG.md
177	            echo "::notice::Restored CHANGELOG preamble ordering (# Changelog → preamble → [Unreleased] → new release → history)"
178	          else
179	            echo "CHANGELOG preamble already at top — no reordering needed"

  "old_string": "      - name: Update marketplace.json version\n        run: |\n          # Targeted JSON edit scoped to .plugins[0].version — resistant to future\n          # schema additions of other `version` keys. Uses a temp file + atomic\n          # rename so a partial-write mid-step cannot leave the file in a broken\n          # shape (jq has no in-place edit). The VERSION variable is interpolated\n          # via jq --arg to avoid shell-quote hazards.\n          VERSION='${{ steps.version.outputs.new_version }}'\n          jq --arg v \"$VERSION\" '.plugins[0].version = $v' .claude-plugin/marketplace.json > .claude-plugin/marketplace.json.tmp\n          mv .claude-plugin/marketplace.json.tmp .claude-plugin/marketplace.json\n          # Sanity check: the written version matches what we set.\n          WROTE=$(jq -r '.plugins[0].version' .claude-plugin/marketplace.json)\n          if [ \"$WROTE\" != \"$VERSION\" ]; then\n            echo \"::error::marketplace.json version write failed: expected $VERSION, got $WROTE\"; exit 1\n          fi",
  "new_string": "      - name: Update marketplace.json version\n        run: |\n          # Targeted JSON edit scoped to .plugins[0].version — resistant to future\n          # schema additions of other `version` keys. Uses a temp file + atomic\n          # rename so a partial-write mid-step cannot leave the file in a broken\n          # shape (jq has no in-place edit). The VERSION variable is interpolated\n          # via jq --arg to avoid shell-quote hazards.\n          VERSION='${{ steps.version.outputs.new_version }}'\n          jq --arg v \"$VERSION\" '.plugins[0].version = $v' .claude-plugin/marketplace.json > .claude-plugin/marketplace.json.tmp\n          mv .claude-plugin/marketplace.json.tmp .claude-plugin/marketplace.json\n          # Sanity check: the written version matches what we set.\n          WROTE=$(jq -r '.plugins[0].version' .claude-plugin/marketplace.json)\n          if [ \"$WROTE\" != \"$VERSION\" ]; then\n            echo \"::error::marketplace.json version write failed: expected $VERSION, got $WROTE\"; exit 1\n          fi\n\n      - name: Update docs/_data/pinned.yaml skf_version\n        env:\n          VERSION: ${{ steps.version.outputs.new_version }}\n        run: |\n          # Keep docs/_data/pinned.yaml.skf_version locked to package.json.version —\n          # pinned.yaml's own header declares this as an invariant and tools/validate-docs-drift.js\n          # enforces it at pre-commit time. This step enforces it at release time so\n          # the invariant is upheld atomically on every version bump.\n          #\n          # Targeted sed: matches ONLY a top-level `skf_version: \"X.Y.Z\"` line. The\n          # VERSION value is passed via env (not ${{ }} interpolation into the script\n          # body) to avoid shell-injection on inputs per Story 5.4 defense-in-depth.\n          sed -i.bak -E 's/^skf_version: \"[^\"]*\"$/skf_version: \"'\"$VERSION\"'\"/' docs/_data/pinned.yaml\n          rm -f docs/_data/pinned.yaml.bak\n          # Sanity check: the written version matches what we set.\n          WROTE=$(grep -E '^skf_version:' docs/_data/pinned.yaml | sed -E 's/^skf_version: \"([^\"]*)\"$/\\1/')\n          if [ \"$WROTE\" != \"$VERSION\" ]; then\n            echo \"::error::docs/_data/pinned.yaml skf_version write failed: expected $VERSION, got $WROTE\"; exit 1\n          fi"

  "command": "gh pr create --base main --head fix/lock-pinned-skf-version-to-package-json --title \"fix(release): lock pinned.yaml.skf_version to package.json.version\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Summary\n\n`docs/_data/pinned.yaml:26` carried stale `skf_version: \"0.10.0\"` while `package.json` and the live v1.0.0 npm release were at `1.0.0`. The file's own header comment declared \"matches the \\`version\\` field in package.json\" as an invariant — but nothing enforced it. **Three-layer defense failure**:\n\n- **Story 5.1 pre-v1.0.0 readiness audit** missed the file in its 8-file envelope sibling-sweep.\n- **`release.yaml` bump-sync set** included `package.json` + `marketplace.json` but not `pinned.yaml`.\n- **`tools/validate-docs-drift.js`** cross-checked `oms-*` skill anchors and the `illustrative_libraries` list but never read `skf_version`.\n\nThis PR closes all three layers in one commit.\n\n## Changes\n\n### 1. `docs/_data/pinned.yaml:26` — bump `0.10.0` → `1.0.0`\n\nReflects current live state (v1.0.0 on npm under `--tag latest` since 2026-04-23T18:56:39Z). Header comment expanded to document the two-layer enforcement introduced below.\n\n### 2. `tools/validate-docs-drift.js` — add `checkSkfVersionAgainstPackageJson()`\n\nReads `package.json.version` and asserts `anchors.skf_version` matches. Runs as part of `npm run quality` (local + pre-commit hook). Not wired into CI `quality.yaml` because `docs:validate-drift` requires the `oh-my-skills` sibling repo checkout, which CI doesn't have — the pre-commit hook is the right enforcement point for these in-tree cross-references.\n\nError message is actionable — it points at the `release.yaml` step name that should have fired, so a future drift tells the maintainer exactly which sync-on-write path failed:\n\n```\nSKF_VERSION_DRIFT: pinned.yaml skf_version \"X.Y.Z\" !== package.json version \"A.B.C\" —\nbump pinned.yaml or check that release.yaml's \"Update pinned.yaml skf_version\" step fired\n```\n\nVerified with negative-case probe (inject `9.9.9`, confirm validator exits 1, restore).\n\n### 3. `.github/workflows/release.yaml` — new \"Update docs/_data/pinned.yaml skf_version\" step\n\nNew step inserted after the `Update marketplace.json version` step. Uses `sed` with a top-level-key anchor (`^skf_version: \"[^\"]*\"$`), VERSION passed via `env:` per Story 5.4 shell-injection defense-in-depth (not interpolated into the script body), and a post-write `grep` sanity-check that `::error::`s on mismatch. Also adds `docs/_data/pinned.yaml` to the `git add` list at the release commit step so the bump rides with `package.json` + `marketplace.json` in the same release commit.\n\n## Pattern coverage\n\nCloses **Epic 5 retro Commitment 13(a)** \"topology claims go stale when the workflow changes\" for this specific invariant. Instantiates **Epic 6 retro Commitment 2** \"symbolic anchor / cross-file validation\" as a tooling guard (not just a prose convention). The two enforcement angles are complementary:\n\n- **Release-side (sync-on-write)** prevents future drift when the version legitimately changes.\n- **Validator-side (CI/pre-commit read-verify)** catches any other path that ever touches the file.\n\n## Test plan\n\n- [x] `node tools/validate-docs-drift.js` positive case — passes with `\"skf_version 1.0.0 matches package.json\"`.\n- [x] Negative-case probe — inject `9.9.9`, confirm validator exits 1 with actionable `SKF_VERSION_DRIFT` message; restore.\n- [x] `npm run quality` — all 13 subcommands exit 0.\n- [x] Pre-commit + commit-msg hooks pass without `--no-verify`.\n- [ ] 7 required CI status checks pass on this PR.\n- [ ] `release.yaml`'s new step will be exercised on the next release cut (v1.0.1 / v1.1.0). Not validated live in this PR — deferred.\n\n## Not bundled\n\n- Any other Epic 5 retro standing flag (P1 rc dist-tag, P2 audit-evidence hardening, P3 NFR9 automation, P7 `package.json.main`).\n- Issue #211 (piped-stdin install infeasibility).\n- Any other file that might theoretically version-drift — scope is strictly `skf_version` because that's the documented invariant in `pinned.yaml`.\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\nEOF\n)\"",

}
